# VinaX

VinaX is a free music web app and Android app built from one codebase and backed by one edge Worker. It plays from a public catalogue, learns a listener's taste, and builds what plays next from it. There are no ads, no subscription and no payment. Listeners sign in with Google; the library, history, taste profile and settings live on the device and sync to the account.

This README is the single document for the repository: what the product is, how the code is organised, how to run, check, release and deploy it, how to operate it, and the reference for each subsystem (database, owner console, Android, VinaX AI, recommendations, design system, privacy) plus a condensed listener guide.

Version source of truth: `frontend/src/constants/version.ts` (`LATEST_VERSION`, `DISPLAY_VERSION`). The current line is **VinaX V2**, which restarted at 1.0.0 after 15.0.1; listeners see "VinaX V2 <major>.<minor>" (for example "VinaX V2 1.11"). The Worker has its own number in `backend/package.json`.

## What a listener gets

- **Sign in with Google.** One tap; the Google photo is the profile picture; library, playlists, history, taste and settings follow the account to every device. Destinations: Home, Search, Library and VinaX AI on a phone's tab bar; Home, Discover, Radio and VinaX AI in the desktop sidebar, with Search as a field in the top bar. On the website, Discover, Charts, Search and every song, album and artist page can be browsed before signing in; play, like and save ask for the account right there.
- **A queue that builds itself.** Tap a song and the queue fills five songs at a time. Radio plays endlessly from a song, artist, mood or station. Tune this queue (Queue page, Now Playing > Up next, Player tools) rebuilds Up Next at once; Familiar, Balanced and Discover set how adventurous the picks are. Never repeat keeps every automatic song one the listener has never heard.
- **A full player.** Reorderable queue (with the songs already played above Now playing), synced lyrics, sleep timer, crossfade (the end of a song plays over the start of the next), sound settings (loudness normalisation brings every song to about the same volume without squashing it), lock-screen and headset controls, casting to a TV (from the website and the Android app), drive mode, disco lights that follow the beat, karaoke (with Vocals: Full, Low or Off), a song's video clip in the artwork card (the clip's own shape where the cover sits, an Artwork / Video chip, a full-screen view with double-tap to like and a quality chip), and a Together tab beside Up next, Lyrics and Credits. Flow (in the sidebar and the phone tab bar) plays music videos one per screen, like reels, and moves to the next when a clip ends; each visit opens on different videos, only in the listener's languages, and videos already watched come back only when nothing new is left; a Videos card or a shared video link opens Flow on that video first.
- **A library on the device.** Favourites, playlists (each ends with Recommended songs from the catalogue), Listen Later, smart collections, history and stats (Your VinaX counts every play this device logged plus the synced history, over 4 weeks, 6 months or All time). One-file backup with preview, merge or replace and Undo; on a new device, signing in with the same account brings it all back.
- **VinaX AI.** Opt-in chat assistant at `/vinaxai` for signed-in listeners: Auto model choice with failover, web search with numbered sources, reasoning display, photos/PDF/text files, artifacts (code, pages, diagrams) in a side panel with safe preview, projects, memory, share links, dictation, Read aloud, Live voice and Create image (a picture from a description, when the owner switches it on). 2.0: ask for a playlist and the reply carries a playlist card (a `vinax-playlist` fenced block the system prompt asks for; `features/vinaxai/PlaylistCard.tsx`, `playlistBlock.ts`) with **Play**, **Add to queue** and **Save to library**; songs are looked up in the catalogue only when one of those is pressed. 2.1 (2026-11-17): one resolver for every card (`createSongResolver` in `suggest.ts`) keeps each title/artist answer (up to 500; a song or "not in the catalogue"; a failed search is not kept), shares a search already running for the same pair and runs at most four searches at once across all cards and presses, so a second button searches nothing. Play starts the first song found in the list's order (`playQueue([first], 0, { keepList: true, from })`, so a one-song start does not turn into radio) and adds the rest with `enqueueAll(batch, { quiet: true })` as each earlier song has answered (`orderedFeed`; up to 50 songs; with shuffle on, the rest go in once, shuffled, after the last answer); it stops adding when another Play press happens or the first song has left the queue, and one toast at the end says how many were not found. When every answer is already known Play plays the whole list in one call. Play and Add to queue ask a signed-out visitor to sign in first. Rows with no match say "Not in the catalogue". `enqueueAll` in `playerStore` takes `{ quiet }` like `enqueue` (its toast now says "1 song").
- **No AI in the music.** Up Next, Radio, Home, playlists (and their Recommended songs) and lyrics are worked out on the device (or by plain catalogue lookups); no listening data goes to an AI service. The music AI (AI DJ, AI order for Up Next, AI-built Home, AI Playlist, AI lyrics tools, song generation) and the server-side personal engine were removed in 1.14. VinaX AI at `/vinaxai` is the one place with AI.
- **Friends and sharing.** Opt-in public profile at `/u/<handle>`, follow listeners ("Friends are listening", and a People tab on `/following` with everyone you follow), Blend (a saved "Blend with <name>" playlist that refreshes when either of you likes something new), shared playlists (public page or invite link; the link follows your account and the shared copy updates itself a few seconds after a change), Listen Together rooms that work like a jam: one shared queue everyone adds to (and takes their own songs out of), play / pause / skip for everyone unless the host keeps it, the host can remove someone, a guest can listen on the host's speaker only with the phone as the remote; plus names, chat and "save this session". **Join a friend's jam:** with the opt-in **Let people I follow join my room**, a room you host shows a **Join** button on Home's Friends are listening row and on your `/u/<handle>` page, only to people who follow each other and only while the room is live.
- **Daily discovery.** Discover 5, Your Week (30 songs new to you, refreshed every Monday), the "Most played on VinaX this week" chart, podcasts that pick up where you left off ("Continue your shows", a Played tick), "Songs like this" with a reason, release alerts for followed artists on the day a song comes out (the `/following` page), a daily listening goal, focus sounds, Auto EQ per song, drive-mode voice commands, playlist import (CSV/M3U/text), smart downloads on Android.
- **Removed in 2.0 (games and rewards).** Badges, listening streaks, weekly goals, the music quiz, the year recap and its story, and per-song reactions are gone. `/quiz` redirects to Discover and `/recap` to Your VinaX; backups no longer carry streak or badge data. For apps that have not updated, `GET /api/reactions` answers an empty count and `POST` 410, and profiles drop any `badges` list sent and always answer `badges: []`. Schema version 25 dropped the `song_reactions` table with its rows, and `song_reaction_counts()` and `stats_reactions()`; `/api/reactions` answers without the database.
- **Looks.** One VinaX look (1.15): black and green, with Theme System (follows the device), Light or Dark (1.15.1) and glass panels over the playing song's colour. There is no accent colour choice; an old true-black theme opens as Dark and Auto as System.
- **Listening moments.** From the play history on the device, VinaX knows what this listener plays at each time of day on weekdays and weekends; Up Next scores those artists and languages higher, Home gets "For your weekday evenings", and the Queue page names the moment. Settings > Recommendations > Time and day awareness switches it off.
- **Ideas & bugs.** The lightbulb in the top bar opens a popup on every platform (Android app, iPhone and iPad web, Windows, Mac, Linux) to suggest a feature or report a bug; the device, app version, screen, language and page are read for the listener and shown before sending; it lands in the console's Feedback inbox.
- **Comfort.** Keyboard shortcuts, reduced motion, high contrast, display size, density, Kid mode, app language (English, Telugu, Hindi, Tamil, Kannada or Malayalam for the menus, Settings, the song menu, the player bar, Now Playing, Search, the welcome sheet, the sign-in pages, empty and error states, snackbars, Help and the guided tours; song names and catalogue text stay as they are), guided tours (eight live tutorials in Help).
- **Android app.** Same app with background playback, notification and lock-screen controls, widgets, offline downloads, push, in-app updates, casting to a TV. iPhone/iPad: Add to Home Screen from Safari (keeps playing with the screen off; sound effects are not available in Safari there). The website and the Home Screen app save songs for offline too (song menu > **Download**, kept in the browser; smart downloads and **Download all** stay Android-only).
- **Device-first data.** A service worker keeps the app shell and downloaded songs available offline. Anonymous usage statistics (which song, city-level location) are shared by default and can be switched off; third-party session insights are off unless turned on. No advertising anywhere.

## Architecture

```text
 Browser or Android app
 +-----------------------------------------------+
 | frontend/  static single-page app             |
 |  stores -- device storage (local, IndexedDB)  |
 |  recommendation engine, audio engine          |
 |  service worker: shell + offline audio        |
 +-----------------------+-----------------------+
                         | same origin (/api, /img, /apk)
 +-----------------------v-----------------------+
 | backend/worker  edge Worker "vinax-api"       |
 |  /api/*: catalogue proxy, sign-in and sync,   |
 |  usage events, rooms, push, status, owner     |
 |  console API, VinaX AI                        |
 |  /img, /apk, host redirects                   |
 |  anything unmatched -> the static site        |
 |  Cron Trigger every 5 min: jobs               |
 +-----------------------+-----------------------+
                         | service-role key (Worker secret)
 +-----------------------v-----------------------+
 | one hosted database (backend/supabase/)       |
 +-----------------------------------------------+
```

| Piece | What it is | How it deploys |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Static frontend | `frontend/` built to `dist/`: a single-page app (every page a lazy chunk), prerendered static routes, song/album/artist/hub pages, the owner console (`/admin/`) and the status page (`/status/`) | The static host's Git integration builds `frontend/` on every push to `main`. No workflow here deploys it (the daily SEO workflow deploys with `wrangler pages deploy`) |
| Worker `vinax-api` | `backend/worker/`: one Worker, no framework. Answers only the routes in `backend/worker/wrangler.toml`; unmatched URLs fall through to the static site (`ASSETS_HOST`) | **Deploy Worker** workflow on a push to `main` touching `backend/**` |
| Database | One hosted Postgres project; schema is `backend/supabase/schema.sql` (tables, `directory` view, `stats_*` and other functions, deny-all row-level security). The Worker is its only client | Paste `schema.sql` into the SQL editor once (idempotent), or let the **Schema** workflow apply it |
| Android package | The same bundle in a generated Capacitor wrapper with a native media service | **Build Android APK** (dispatched by **Auto release**), **Release APK** on a `v*` tag |
| VinaX AI sandbox | `sandbox/`: one-page static site on its own origin for artifact previews | Separate static project `vinax-sandbox` |

The two halves deploy separately and either can fail alone. The frontend holds no secrets; `VITE_*` variables are visible to every visitor. Row-level security is forced on every table with no policy, so only the Worker's service-role key can read or write; the app and console never hold a database key.

Main packages: `react` 19, `react-router-dom` 6, `zustand` 4 (persist), `@tanstack/react-query` 5, `tailwindcss` 3 plus hand-written token CSS, built with `vite` 8 and `typescript` 5; `@capacitor/*` 8 for Android; `wrangler` 4 for the Worker; `vitest` 4, `@playwright/test` and `@axe-core/playwright` for tests. The Worker reaches the database over its REST interface with `fetch` (no client library).

### App shell (`frontend/src/main.tsx`)

1. Imports `services/storage/earlyMigrations` first so the leftover keys of removed features are erased before stores rehydrate. (The old `tarang.*` to `vinax.*` key move is gone: this origin and the `app.Vinax.music` app started with empty storage. Backups with `app: 'tarang'` and the `tarang-db` IndexedDB name are still read.)
2. Renders `<App />` and loads global CSS in order: `styles/index.css` (tokens, base), `shell.css` (frame), `features.css`, `templates/index.css` (app styles). Page styles in `styles/pages/` ship with their lazy page.
3. Removes `boot-still` after the first painted frame; on `vite:preloadError` reloads once per session (not offline).
4. Sets `device-phone|tablet|desktop|tv` and `pointer-coarse|pointer-fine` classes on `<html>`.
5. Registers `/sw.js` in production web builds, then loads `utils/precache.ts` with `import()` (outside the first load), which asks it to precache on boot, when the network returns and when a new worker takes over. It asks for the full asset graph, except with Settings > Data saver or the browser's data saver (`navigator.connection.saveData`) on a metered or unknown connection: then it asks for the light precache (the shell, the layout's chunks, and the `/offline`, Library and Now Playing pages); other pages are cached the first time they open. On Wi-Fi or ethernet it still takes everything, the Android app always does, and it asks for the full one as soon as the connection turns unmetered or Data saver is turned off.

`src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx` is the frame for every route: sidebar (from 768 px; 80 px rail below 1100 px), top bar, routed page in an error boundary, the player (compact card above the four-destination tab bar on phones/tablets; floating deck from 1024 px), Now Playing panel on wide workspaces, snackbars, welcome sheet, command palette, and (only while a Listen Together session is live) the lazy `TogetherController`. It runs the one-time bootstrap (storage migrations, `initEngine()`, downloads, consent-gated telemetry, lock-screen lyrics, TV spatial navigation, alarm, output watcher, and in the app the update check; casting is not started here, see [Audio engine and media session](#audio-engine-and-media-session)). Three of these load on demand, outside first load: `services/tv/spatialNav` (dynamic import on every device; it acts only on a TV), the alarm (`services/alarm/boot.ts` `bootAlarm`: in the app it always loads and starts, so the wake notification stays in step; on the web it loads only once an alarm is stored or turned on) and the update gate (`services/update`, `store/updateStore` and `@capacitor/app` together, app only, with the resume re-check), owns scroll memory and hardware-back handling, and hides its chrome on `/vinaxai*` and Now Playing.

### Routing and lazy chunks

`src/router/index.tsx` builds one browser router; every page is `lazy()`. `/vinaxai`, `/vinaxai/c/:id`, `/vinaxai/projects`, `/vinaxai/project/:id`, `/vinaxai/artifacts` and `/vinaxai/share/:id` are VinaX AI. `/VinaXAI` redirects to `/vinaxai` in the router (not `_redirects`: a rule there could match `/vinaxai` and loop); the retired AI pages `/ai-playlist/*` and `/ai/shared/*` go Home (`frontend/public/_redirects` 301s them too); `/handoff` (the retired Move to a new device, with any `?c=` or `?mode=receive`) goes to `/settings#your-data`; `/explore` and `/festivals` go to `/discover` and `/regions` to `/languages` (Explore folded into Discover and Regions into Languages; `_redirects` 301s them to `/discover/` and `/languages/` too); `/mixes` goes to `/made-for-you` (Mixes showed the same mixes as Made for you; it was never prerendered, so only the router redirects); `/video/:id` goes to `/flow?v=<id>` (`VideoToFlow` in the router; the separate video player page is gone and Flow is the one video player; `/video/<id>` was never prerendered or in a sitemap, so there is no `_redirects` line). `/share` (`pages/SharePage.tsx`; signed in like every page in `AppLayout`; `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` in `_headers`) is **Share to VinaX**: the installed web app's `share_target` (`manifest.webmanifest`: GET with `title`, `text` and `url`; installed Chromium apps on Android and computers, not iPhone) and the Android app's share sheet both land there, and `shareAction()` in `features/share/shareTarget.ts` (pure) decides: a VinaX link (www or apex, never `/share` itself) opens its page; two or more lines open Import a playlist (`ImportPlaylistSheet` with `initialText`; at most 100 lines, links and junk lines dropped; saving or closing goes to `/library`); a JioSaavn link searches the name in its slug; one line goes to `/search/<query>` without another app's wrapper ("Check out ... on Spotify"; "Title - Artist" is read as "Title Artist"); otherwise the share's title is searched; with nothing left the page says "Nothing to look up" and links to Search. The manifest's `theme_color` and `background_color` are `#000000`, like `index.html`. Language hub pages (`/<language>-songs`) and mood hubs (`/<language>-<mood>-songs`) come from `constants/languages.ts` and `constants/hubs.ts`. An offline boot with saved songs, in the Android app or the installed web app (standalone), opens `/offline`; the "Startup page" setting can open Search, Library or the last main route. `vite.config.ts` groups first-load code into `router`, `vendor`, `data` and `core` chunks; `core` lists small modules already in the first-load graph by explicit path (see [Checks and testing](#checks-and-testing)).

### The look pipeline

`settingsStore` (`vinax.settings.v1`: `theme`, `template`, `glassLevel`, `glassBlur`, `highContrast`, `uiScale`, `density`, `reduceMotion`) -> before first paint an inline script in `frontend/index.html` resolves the theme (System follows `prefers-color-scheme`), sets `.light`/`.dark`, `data-template`, the glass dials and the canvas colour -> at run time an effect in `AppLayout` calls `applyThemeClasses(root, template, resolveTheme(theme, systemDark))` (`src/utils/theme.ts`) and follows the device live while the setting is System. 1.15 removed the accent, custom accent and dynamic theme (settings v6); 1.15.1 brought the theme back as System / Light / Dark (settings v7: a pre-1.15 choice is kept, Black → Dark, Auto → System). Details in [Design system](#design-system).

### Stores and persistence

| Store | Key | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `playerStore` | `vinax.player.v1` | queue, index, repeat, shuffle, volume, muted, rate |
| `settingsStore` | `vinax.settings.v1` | every preference |
| `libraryStore` | `vinax.library.v1` | favourites, playlists, Listen Later, saved albums/artists, hidden songs |
| `historyStore` | `vinax.history.v1` | last 150 plays |
| `searchStore`, `searchWorkspaceStore` | `vinax.search.v1`, `vinax.search.workspace.v1` | recent, pinned, saved searches |
| `smartCollectionStore`, `bookmarkStore`, `downloadsStore`, `alarmStore`, `outputStore`, `lyricsOffsetStore` | their own `vinax.*` keys | what the names say |

The taste profile is not a store: `src/services/personalization/` reads/writes `vinax.profile.v1` (and `vinax.profile.kid.v1` in Kid mode). `src/constants/storage-keys.ts` is the key registry; the listen-event log is in IndexedDB (`services/storage/idb.ts`). **Your VinaX** (`pages/StatsPage.tsx`) reads up to 5000 of its events (`LOG_LIMIT`) and merges their plays with the synced History (`features/stats/playLog.ts`): `logPlays()` keeps the `play` rows and takes completed/skipped and heard time from the complete or skip row that follows; `mergePlays()` counts each play once (a log play is the History entry with the same song id logged 5 s before to 30 min after it, nearest first, so a replay still counts twice); History is the authority for the stretch it covers, so a log play there without an entry was removed and is dropped; log plays older than History's oldest entry fill in what the 150-play cap cut off only while History is nearly full (>= 140 entries), so a cleared History stays cleared; History's own entries keep plays from other devices in. Ranges (`STATS_RANGES`): 4 weeks (default, remembered for the tab in `vinax.stats.range.v1`), 6 months, All time; the weekly report and the 12-week calendar keep their own windows over the merged plays. `mergedCoverageNote()` says how far back the plays on record go and, signed in, from when older plays are this device's only. History's clears do not touch the log, so each clear writes a note to `vinax.stats.forgotten.v1` (`features/stats/forgotten.ts`: clear all = `{from: 0, to: now}`, Clear last hour/today = from that time, a removed play = that song for 30 minutes; Undo restores the earlier notes) and the merge skips log plays inside them; `staleClearNote()` adds one for clears made before the notes existed. All persisted stores write through `services/storage/local.ts`:

- **Guarded storage:** reads never throw; a failed write shows one toast per session ("Storage is full ...").
- **Write freeze:** a restore writes straight to `localStorage` then reloads; `freezeLocalWrites()` drops every store-driven write until the reload so live stores cannot persist old state over restored keys. Only `writeLocalBatch()` (all-or-nothing, rolls back on failure) still writes.
- **De-duplicated player persistence:** `createDedupedStorage()` skips writes when no persisted field changed by reference (progress ticks do not write). On rehydrate the player store treats its record as untrusted (malformed songs dropped, scalars clamped).
- **Shared playlist links** (`features/social/shareMap.ts`): `vinax.shared-playlists.v1` maps each local playlist id to its `/p/` copy `{ id, version, public?, at, off? }` and travels in the `library` backup category and so in the account sync. The invite token never sits in the map: it lives in the device-only `vinax.shared-playlist-tokens.v1` (keyed by shared id, in no backup; any owner write with collab on gets it back from the server). `migrateShareTokens()` moves tokens an older build kept inside the map, from `installAccountSync` before the first sync round and from a restore that includes the library. Stopping a share leaves an `off` mark (dropped after 180 days) so a merge with an older copy cannot bring the link back; a merge keeps, per playlist, the entry with the newer `at` (this device wins a tie) and never lowers a version. `features/social/sharedCopy.ts` `pushSharedCopy()` is the only write path: it sends the version this account last wrote, so a friend's newer songs answer `409` and the merge re-reads the copy, writes their version plus the extra songs and brings the extras into the local playlist; a missing copy or a `404` drops the link (`gone`); with `onlyIfChanged` a copy with the same name, description and song order is not written again. `installSharedAutoPush()` (dynamic import from `installAccountSync`) pushes a linked playlist 5 s after its last change (`AUTO_PUSH_DELAY_MS`, one push at a time per playlist; nothing signed out or in read-only mode) and toasts when collaborators' songs came in.
- **Blends** (`features/social/blend.ts`, `BlendPanel.tsx`, used only by the lazy `ProfilePage`): `vinax.blends.v1` keeps, per friend's handle, the playlist id, their profile's `updatedAt`, keys of the seeds used on each side (`theirsKey`, yours), when it was built and whose likes each song came from; device only (not in the backup or the sync; the playlist itself syncs like any playlist). A blend takes up to 10 of their `recentLikes` and 10 of your favourites in batches of five seeds, four `recommendFromSeeds` calls of 10 picks run at once (their batches with `languages: []`, every like excluded from the picks); `mixBlend` puts songs you both like first, then alternates their side and yours, leaves out hidden songs and artists and stops at 50 (`BLEND_MAX`). `saveBlend` creates a normal playlist "Blend with <name>" (tag `blend`) through `libraryStore.createCollection`; a refresh replaces its songs in place and keeps songs added by hand. `needsRefresh` compares the seed keys (`updatedAt` moves on every profile write, so it is only a first cheap check); the profile rebuilds once per visit when they differ. `blendFor` returns null once the playlist is deleted.
- **Podcast episode places** (`features/podcasts/progress.ts`, `episode.ts`): episodes resume through the same 80-entry `vinax.resume.v1` map as songs, but a full map drops its oldest entry that is not an episode (`resumeEvictee()`), so music never pushes an episode's place out. `playerStore.saveResume` hands an episode to `progress.ts` by dynamic import (only `episode.ts`, a few hundred bytes, is in first load); `vinax.podcast.progress.v1` keeps up to 50 episodes (least recently heard dropped; the song, seconds in, length, last heard, played), device only. An episode is played at 95%, or 6 s before the end when that comes first (the player saves every 5 s); its resume entry is then removed so a replay starts from the top, and replaying it past 20 s turns it back to in progress. `useEpisodeProgress()` follows changes live (a window event and the `storage` event).

### Catalogue client (`src/services/api/client.ts`)

Catalogue reads go through `orchestratedRequest()`. Pages use `@tanstack/react-query` with `retry: 0`, 5-minute stale time, 30-minute cache time (`services/queryClient.ts`) because retrying is the orchestrator's job.

- **Bases** (`constants/endpoints.ts`): a dedicated catalogue API host, the same-origin Worker catalogue `/api/cat` (web only), and the production origin's `/api/cat`. `VITE_API_BASES` replaces the list at build time; the console can switch bases off.
- **Ranking:** each base by expected time to success (latency EWMA + failure odds x failure cost, stretched per consecutive failure). Three failures in a row cool a base 60 s; all are still tried when every one is cooling.
- **Priority:** `interactive` (Search, typeahead, `getSong`) best base first, hedged to the next base after the base's own p75 (350-900 ms), first valid answer wins, loser aborted and not a failure; `background` (shelves, recs, prefetch; default for searches) first base drawn at random weighted by health, never hedged; `standard` best first, no hedge.
- **Back-off:** 429/503 cool a base for `Retry-After` (30 s if missing, clamped 1 s-10 min). At most six requests in flight per base; interactive callers queue ahead of background.
- **Soft misses:** a payload the validator rejects, or HTTP 404, tries the next path dialect with no health strike. Up to two full passes with a 600 ms pause.
- **Memo:** `cacheMs` (5 s for searches and song lookups) shares identical in-flight calls and reuses answers (40 kept).
- **Deadlines:** 8 s per attempt (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), 20 s for the ladder (`REQUEST_DEADLINE_MS`). Cancellation via `AbortSignal` never counts as failure.
- **Boot prefetch:** `index.html` starts the first trending request and parks the promise on `window.__vxBoot`.
- Payloads are normalised in `services/api/normalize.ts` (API `artists: { primary: [...] }` -> app `artists: [...]`).

### Audio engine and media session

`src/services/audio/engine.ts` exports one `audioEngine` owning a single `<audio>` element; the player store is its only consumer. Sources are ordered by bitrate against the quality preference (downloaded copy first); a failing source advances to the next variant, a source that had been playing gets one same-position retry. Stall timer, fades, rate, `setSinkId` and next-track preloading live here. Fades set the volume the clock says on every tick (`rampAt`, also driven from `timeupdate`), so in a hidden tab or with the screen off they keep moving and end on time instead of jumping to the target. **Crossfade:** `overlap(ms)` moves the playing element aside as a tail that fades to 0 over `ms` and no longer reports `ended`/`pause` to the store, and makes the gapless preload element the playing one (its buffered data is used when it holds that song; otherwise the next song loads as usual); the next song fades in over the same time. It refuses (returns false) when paused, on iPhone Safari (`iosWeb()`: `el.volume` cannot be set) or while the Web Audio chain is on or still attached (sound effects, disco lights, karaoke vocals); the song then only fades out. Another `load()` (a skip), `pause()`, `destroy()` stop the tail at once, and `setMuted` mutes it too; effects (`effects.ts`) can be bypassed. The Web Audio chain runs for either of two reasons: Sound effects (`setEffectsEnabled`) or disco lights and karaoke vocals (`setAnalysisEnabled`, which runs it flat through `setProcessing(false)` so the song sounds unchanged); a flat chain left when the lights or karaoke close comes off before the next song loads, so closing them never interrupts the song.

**The chain** (`services/audio/effects.ts`, lazy): source -> vocals -> 5-band EQ -> mono -> balance -> level (the song's loudness gain) -> limiter -> analyser, plus a dead-end loudness meter tapped off the vocals output. **Karaoke vocals** (`setVocals`, `vocalMix`; Full / Low / Off) is the first stage, because it needs the stereo exactly as the file holds it: a voice mixed dead centre cancels in the side signal (L - R), and a 180 Hz low-passed mono copy (`VOCAL_BASS_HZ`) brings the centred bass and kick back. Full is the song unchanged; Off is the side signal in both speakers plus the bass copy; Low blends the song with the side signal in anti-phase so the voice sits about 9 dB down and the stereo stays balanced (on a mono speaker Low only lowers the whole song). A mono song goes almost silent with Off. Mode changes glide over about 50 ms, so switching never clicks. The vocals stage is not one of the listener's sound effects: it runs even when the chain is flat. `/karaoke` (`features/karaoke/VocalsControl.tsx`, in the lazy karaoke chunk) asks for the chain through `audioEngine.setAnalysisEnabled(true)` only the first time Low or Off is picked (the engine reloads the song in CORS mode at the same spot, as for disco lights), so opening karaoke alone changes nothing; leaving puts the vocals back to Full and hands the chain back. `features/karaoke/vocals.ts` (`vocalsBlocked`) gives the one line shown when it cannot work: iPhone or iPad Safari, a browser without Web Audio, casting, or a source the chain bypassed as tainted (only one this page asked for). Tests: `services/audio/effectsVocals.test.ts` (a fake AudioContext: wiring, what each mode sounds like), `features/karaoke/vocals.test.ts`, `pages/KaraokePage.test.tsx`.

**Loudness levelling** (Settings > Sound > Loudness normalisation, `normalize`; the maths and the cache in `services/audio/loudness.ts`, imported only by `effects.ts`). Each song as a whole is moved toward about -14 LUFS, by at most +/-9 dB (`TARGET_LUFS`, `MAX_LEVEL_DB`); its dynamics are not squashed. The meter is K-weighted like BS.1770: a +4 dB high shelf at 1.5 kHz, a 38 Hz high-pass, then its own analyser (`fftSize` 16384, blocks of about 0.34 s), gated at -70 LUFS absolute and -10 LU relative. While a song's loudness is unknown a block is read every 400 ms and counts only while the song audibly plays (clock moving, not muted, volume >= 0.2 and steady, not mid-fade, vocals Full, context running); the element volume is taken back out of the reading. After 3 blocks the level glides toward the estimate (time constant 1.5 s); after 25 (about 10 s) the value is stored for that song id and metering stops. `vinax.loudness.v1` keeps the last 500 songs (LRU; every read and write in try/catch, the in-memory copy survives blocked storage, nothing written while writes are frozen); a replay gets its gain at once, on attach or on a track change on the same element (`engine.ts` passes `trackId` to `attachEffects`), and a song heard for the first time starts from the median of known songs once 5 are stored (`priorLoudness()`). The compressor is only a peak limiter (`LIMITER`: threshold -1.5 dBFS, knee 0, ratio 20, attack 1 ms, release 100 ms); the level gain takes off the compressor's own automatic makeup gain (`LIMITER_MAKEUP_DB`), so the overall level lands on the target and a +9 dB boost on a full-scale peak stays below 0 dBFS. With normalisation or Sound effects off the level is 0 dB, the compressor transparent and nothing is metered. Tests: `loudness.test.ts`, `effectsLoudness.test.ts`.

**Casting** (`services/cast/`, shared by the website and the Android app). `index.ts` is the only first-load part: the cast store (`available`, `connected`, `deviceName`, `native`, `nearby`), the `CastTransport` both senders implement, `castLoadSong` (the player store's `startTrack` calls it while casting), `castRequestSession` and the player's intercepts (play or pause with the wanted state, seek, volume go to the receiver). `media.ts` (`castMediaFor`, `castMime`) is the one place a receiver's media is built for both senders: the best network (http/https) stream in the quality order, title, artist and network artwork, never the inline placeholder. Nothing Cast starts at app start: `ensureCastSdk()` runs the first time Connect to a device (`components/DeviceSheet.tsx`) opens and loads `web.ts` plus the vendor's sender script on the web, or `native.ts` in the Android app (the `VinaxCast` plugin, see [Native layer](#native-layer); it does nothing in an app built before the plugin, where `Capacitor.isPluginAvailable('VinaxCast')` is false, or when the plugin answers unavailable). `session.ts` (lazy, both senders): a session start mutes the phone first, then hands the song over where it is (the volume comes back if the load fails); a session end gives the volume back. While casting, the phone's own `<audio>` plays muted as the clock that moves the queue on: a pause or play on the TV pauses or plays that clock, and a TV position more than 3 s (`CAST_DRIFT_S`) from it while playing seeks it; both act only on reports about the stream last sent, after a 2.5 s quiet window following any load, seek or play/pause from the phone, and only on a change. Both senders use the Default Media Receiver. The website shows the vendor's cast button in the player bar and Now Playing; the Android app shows a TV button in Now Playing once a TV is nearby or a session is live ("Cast to a TV" / "Casting to <name>"), and "Cast to a TV" in Connect to a device opens the phone's device list (while casting, the TV's card with Stop casting). Tests: `services/cast/cast.test.ts`, `src/__tests__/patchAndroidCast.test.ts`. **The Android side has not been compiled or run on a device yet** (see [What cannot be verified without a device](#what-cannot-be-verified-without-a-device)).

### Disco lights

`/disco` (`pages/DiscoPage.tsx`, from the player's ⋯ menu) turns the screen into a light show that follows the beat. `features/disco/beat.ts` (pure, unit-tested) reads each spectrum frame from the chain's analyser (`readSpectrum` in `effects.ts`): a beat is a rise in the 40-160 Hz band above its own one-second average times a threshold set by the listener's sensitivity (1.6x to 1.15x), above a floor, at least 280 ms after the last; the tempo is the median gap of recent beats, folded into 70-180. `features/disco/lights.ts` draws four styles on a canvas (Party, Spotlights, Lasers, and Wash: the whole screen in full-strength colour with two more colours drifting across it as glowing clouds, brighter on the beat) in six colour sets (the cover's colour via `extractVibrantColor`, RGB, Rainbow, Neon, Warm, Cool); Brightness goes to 150%. Tapping the lights to hide the controls also asks for full screen, hiding the phone's status and navigation bars. Where the music cannot be read (iPhone Safari, casting, a source the chain hears as silence for 3 s) the lights keep a steady rhythm: the last tempo heard, else 120, or what the listener taps. The white flash is off by default, never fires closer than 340 ms (under three a second) and never under reduced motion; the wash changes colour at most twice a second. The phone itself (`features/disco/phone.ts`): the flashlight button (also a switch in Adjust) flashes the flashlight on each beat through `TorchPulser` (on for 110 ms, never closer than 340 ms), by the Android app's `VinaxDisco` plugin (`native-android/DiscoPlugin.java`: `CameraManager.setTorchMode`, no camera opened, no permission; off when the app is paused) or, elsewhere, the camera track's `torch` constraint (Chrome on Android, Safari on recent iPhones; asks for the camera once, records nothing, stops the camera when switched off); devices without either say so. In the Android app the same plugin holds the window at full brightness while the lights are open and hides the system bars while the controls are hidden (the web view ignores the browser's full screen; browsers use it); everything is put back on leaving. Settings are per device in `vinax.disco.v1` (`features/disco/discoStore.ts`), in no backup. `services/media-session/index.ts` publishes metadata/state/position to the browser media session or, on Android, to the `VinaXMedia` plugin (see [Android](#android)). `services/native/index.ts` bridges platform checks, notification permission, download/update helpers.

### Listen Together

| Piece | File | Role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Session store | `services/together/session.ts` (`useTogether`) | mode `idle\|host\|guest`, room code, status `connecting\|live\|reconnecting\|host-away`, queue, drift, `needsTap`; mode and code kept in `sessionStorage` (`vinax.together.session.v1`) so a reload rejoins |
| Controller | `features/together/TogetherController.tsx` | lazy; shows the Live pill on every page except `/together` (draggable: its spot is kept as a share of the screen in `vinax.together-pill.v1`) and, on every page, the reactions rising over the whole screen (`Sky`, portalled to `<body>`) |
| Engine | `features/together/engine.ts` | `runSession`, `startHosting`, `joinSession`, `leaveSession`, `addSong`, `react`, `say`, `typing`, `tapToListen`; the jam (1.14): `control`, `takeOut` / `canTakeOut`, `setGuestControl`, `removeFromRoom`, `setOnThisPhone` |
| Live line | `features/together/live.ts` (`LiveLine`, `cleanLive`) | One WebSocket per phone to Supabase Realtime (Broadcast) on the channel `vx-room-<code>`: the Phoenix channel protocol written out (join, heartbeat, broadcast), reconnecting by itself; no client library |
| Room extras | `features/together/room.ts` (`useRoom`), `Chat.tsx` | Lazy store: chat lines in flight, who is typing, reactions in the air, the room's energy; `chatRows`, `typingText` (pure, unit-tested) |
| Sync | `features/together/sync.ts` | `ServerClock`, `HostClock`, `decideCorrection`, `RateNudge`, `SeekLead`, `hostIsAway`, `ReactionFeed` (pure, unit-tested) |
| Invites | `features/together/invite.ts` | `inviteUrl` (what the QR encodes), `codeFromScan`, `appIntentUrl`, `deepLinkPath` (pure, unit-tested); `useInviteQr.ts` draws the QR on the device |
| Join | `features/together/JoinRoom.tsx`, `QrScanSheet.tsx` | Room code field plus "Scan the host's QR": the camera runs only while the sheet is open, read by `BarcodeDetector` or the lazy `jsqr` fallback. Needs `camera=(self)` in `public/_headers` and `CAMERA` in the APK (`scripts/patch-android.js`) |
| Player tab | `features/together/TogetherPanel.tsx` | The Together tab of `/now-playing`: start a room on the playing song, join, or (live) code, QR, share, open the room, and (1.14) the room's chat. While a room is live the Live pill is not shown on `/now-playing` (it sat on the chat's Send button) unless a guest's audio needs a tap |
| Website to app | `features/together/OpenInApp.tsx` | Android browsers only: an `intent://` link that opens the room in the app (package `app.Vinax.music`), falling back to `/download`. The APK's `MainActivity` takes `https://(www.)vinaxmusic.online` links of the pages people share (`/song/`, `/album/`, `/artist/`, `/playlist/`, `/video/` (kept for older shared video links, which the app sends to Flow), `/flow` (a shared video is `/flow?v=<id>`; `pathPrefix` matches the path only), `/podcast/`, `/lyrics/`, `/p/`, `/u/`, `/together`, `/vinaxai/share/`; Home, the sign-in pages and Get the app stay in the browser) as verified App Links on www: `frontend/public/.well-known/assetlinks.json` names the release key's SHA-256 and the www filter has `autoVerify` (`patch-android.js`). The apex has its own filter without `autoVerify`, because the "Apex to www" rule redirects its `assetlinks.json` and Android refuses a redirected one; Android 12+ verifies www on its own, while Android 11 and older keep the chooser unless `/.well-known/*` on the apex is exempted from that rule; `AppLayout` reads them with `App.getLaunchUrl` / `appUrlOpen` and navigates |
| Room API | `services/together/index.ts` -> `GET`/`POST /api/room` | create, update, request, heartbeat, react, leave (also `sendBeacon`), end; `sendControl`, `removePerson` (1.14) |

**How two phones stay together.** Both the host and every guest keep the server's clock (`ServerClock`): each answer from `/api/room` carries `now` (the server's clock when the request arrived) and `took` (ms it then spent on the database), so a device knows the network's share of the round trip, and the answer with the shortest trip among the last 40 sets the clock (a slow answer changes nothing; an error both phones share cancels out). The host reads the playhead from the audio element (not the store's `timeupdate` copy) and pushes it with `at`, the server moment it was read at, which the Worker stores as `updated_at` (`pushMoment`: accepted from 5 s before to 0.1 s after the push's arrival). The app sends `at` with every push once it knows the server's clock; a push without it (before the host's first poll) is stamped on arrival and the next pushes replace it. The update answer's `took` and `stamps: true` stay only for installed apps up to 1.16.2, which wait for `stamps` before sending `at`; an answer without `now` is skipped (the pre-10.0 fallback is gone). So how long any one message takes no longer matters. The host pushes again when its audio is really moving after a play, a song change, a seek or a stall, and once more two seconds in; the guest's `HostClock` lets the first two pushes after such a jump each replace the one before, then moves half way per push (a gap over 0.25 s is a new jump). A song starts at the host's position (`playSong(song, { startAt })`, never the device's own resume point); a start more than 0.25 s off gets one early seek; a seek aims ahead by what seeks have been costing (`SeekLead`); a pause steps back to the host's exact spot and a play starts where the host is by then; answers that arrive out of order are dropped. Four times a second `RateNudge` plays up to 8 % faster or slower (pitch kept) from 12 ms off until within 4 ms; beyond 0.45 s it seeks. While following, the guest's own playback speed is set aside and comes back on leaving. **Fine-tune this phone** (guest room page, `setTrimMs`, +/- 2000 ms since 1.14 (was 500) in 10 ms and 100 ms steps, kept per device in `vinax.together-trim.v1`, in no backup) is for what no clock can measure: how long a phone's speaker or a Bluetooth one takes to sound. Not covered: a host playing at a speed other than 1x. `node scripts/together-measure.mjs` (after `npm run build`) runs a host and a guest in two Chrome sessions with phone-like delay and jitter and prints guest minus host every half second: 1.12.10 settled 40 to 75 ms apart and took 14 s to recover from a pause; 1.12.11 holds 2 to 20 ms and recovers in about 3 s.

**The live line (instant play, pause, chat, typing, reactions).** Polling alone made everything wait for a phone's next poll (2 s for a guest, 4 s for the host). `/api/room` (`create` and every `GET`) now also answers `live: { url, key }`: Supabase Realtime's address and the project's publishable key, from the Worker vars `REALTIME_URL` and `REALTIME_KEY` (`liveLine` hands out only a `wss://<ref>.supabase.co/realtime/v1` address and a public key: `sb_publishable_...` or a JWT whose role is `anon`). Each phone in a room opens one socket to it (`LiveLine`; allowed by `wss://*.supabase.co` in the CSP `connect-src`) and broadcasts: `state` (host only sends it: song stub, position, playing, and `at`, the server moment the playhead was read at, sent the instant something changes and with every push), `chat` (`id`, `by`, `text`), `typing` (`by`, `on`), `react` (`e`, `by`) and `poke` (read the room now: after a guest's request or skip vote). A guest takes a `state` with `HostClock.pushed` (no round trip needed: the server's clock is known from the polls) and follows at once: `decideCorrection`'s `urgent` lets a play, a pause or a jump of more than 2 s through without the 3 s cooldown, and a play heard within 0.15 s of the host starts in place with no seek. The line is a shortcut, never the truth: every write still goes to `/api/room` and every phone still polls it. For 5 s after a `state`, a poll whose `updated_at` is older than it does not undo it (the host's push is still on its way); after that the server wins, which also bounds what a prankster holding the room code can do with the line to a blip. A `state` stamped more than 0.4 s ahead of, or 15 s behind, the server's clock is not acted on (the guest polls instead). The same reading heard on the line and then read in a poll counts once (`HostClock`, readings within 2 ms). Everything arriving on the line is validated like any stranger's input; a song on the line is only what a room shows (never a stream address). The public key reads no table (`schema.sql` denies the anon role everything). Without the vars, with the socket blocked, or against an older app, rooms behave exactly as before. A chat line carries an id its sender chose: it is on the sender's screen at once (`sending`), goes down the line and to `say` (which keeps a retried id once), and the copy in flight is dropped when the server lists it; a line neither took is marked and can be sent again as the same line. The ids a tab has sent are kept in `sessionStorage` (`vinax.together.sent.v1`) so they stay "mine" after a reload. Reactions fill the room's energy (`ENERGY_FULL` 10, one step fades every 1.5 s); a full bar sets the room off (everyone's emoji over every screen, at most every 15 s). Guests also look up the next song in the queue ahead of time, so a song change costs only the audio. **Asking for a song.** A guest asks from the room's search box or by tapping (or queueing) any song anywhere in the app: while a device follows a host the room's engine registers `setRoomAsk` on the player store, and `playQueue` / `enqueue` / `enqueueNext` hand the song to `ask()` instead of playing it (the engine's own plays carry `opts.room`). `ask()` sends nothing for the song playing now, one already up next, or one asked for in the last minute. The request goes to `/api/room` (`request`) and a `poke` on the line makes the host read it at once; the host's app looks the full song up, adds it to Up next and shows who asked with **Play now** (`playNow`, also on every Up next row of the host's room page).

**The jam (1.14, schema 22).** Listen Together works like Spotify's Jam. Everyone adds songs straight into the shared queue (no asking) and each Up next row says who added it, with a cross to take it out for the host (any song) and for the guest who added it. Guests play, pause, skip and go back for everyone: the room's buttons, the player bar, the lock screen and the keyboard all go through the player store's `setRoomControl` hook while a device follows a host (the engine's own plays run inside `asRoom`, so they are not sent back to the room); a tap while this phone is out of step with the room (the browser held its audio) only puts this phone right. The host's switch **Guests can control playback** (on by default, sent with every push as `guestControl`) keeps playback to itself; guests then get the old vote-to-skip button. The host can remove someone (the cross by their name, then Remove). A guest's switch **Play on this phone**, turned off, keeps this phone quiet so only the host's speaker plays, and the phone is the remote. The host's app still owns the music: a guest's command goes to `/api/room` (`control`: `play`, `pause`, `next`, `prev`, `add`, `remove`, with an id so it is done once), which checks the sender is not removed and, for playback, that guests may control it, and appends it atomically (`room_control`, the newest 20 within two minutes); a `poke` on the live line makes the host read it at once, and the host carries it out (a playback command older than 10 s, or an add older than 90 s, is not: a host that reloads must not replay a pause). The room shows people by a handle, the first 12 hex of SHA-256(code:deviceId), never the device id (which heartbeats and votes are counted by): `GET ...&member=<deviceId>` answers `people: [{ h, name }]`, `me` and `removed`; the host's poll also gets `controls`; `room.guestControl` and `room.hostH` (the host's handle, from the `deviceId` the host sends with `update`) tell every phone the settings and who the host is. `kick` (host token, `who` handle) adds the member to `jam.removed`: they are no longer counted, shown, heard or allowed to control, and their next poll tells them. A host app older than 1.14 sends no `deviceId`, so `hostH` is null and guests fall back to asking (`request`) and vote-to-skip. **Unread chat (1.14).** A chat line from someone else that arrives while no chat is on screen (the room page or the player's Together tab, `openChat` while `Chat` is mounted) adds to `useTogether.unread`, shown as a count on the Live pill and on the player's Together tab until a chat opens. What the room said before this phone joined is not counted (the first server list is taken as read: `noteChat` in `room.ts`); a line heard on the live line and then listed by the server counts once (by its id); this phone's own lines never count. The count starts afresh on a reload. **Sync check (1.14).** A guest's room page shows how far this phone's player is from the host's right now (`drift`, updated by the fine sync four times a second, not only per poll), whether the instant connection (live line) is up, and the app version; **Resync** (`resync`) starts the host's clock afresh, reads the room at once and puts the phone where the host is. Every heartbeat carries `app` (the version) and `sync` (`SYNC_PROTOCOL`, now 2; raise it when older apps can no longer keep in step); `people` carry both, so a member with none (an app older than 1.14) or a lower protocol gets an **Old app** tag and the room says whose VinaX to update; a phone that is itself behind the others is told so. Added after a real test in which guests played seconds behind on every kind of device while the measured sync (`together-measure.mjs`, desktop and phone emulation, against the live room API) held within 20 ms: an out-of-date app on one device was the likeliest cause, and the check makes the next test say. `v100-together.spec.ts` walks the jam with two browser contexts against an in-memory room: the guest pauses, plays, adds, takes out and skips for everyone, then turns off Play on this phone (switch off, the remote line, audio paused, Sync check "Not playing here (remote only)" with Resync disabled, the host still playing and still listing the guest), works the host's speaker with Pause / Play for everyone while staying quiet as the host plays on, and turns the switch back on; then the host keeps control or removes the guest. Known edge: the player keeps one copy of a song, played ones included, so a song that already played this session is not added again (the host is told). Removed members can still speak on the live line until the host ends the room (it carries no identity); the server ignores them.

**Join a friend's jam (schema 23).** Settings > Profile > **Let people I follow join my room** (`profiles.share_jam`, off by default, shown once a profile exists; only the owner gets it back, as `shareJam`). While this device hosts a room with the switch on, `SocialBridge` (lazy Bridges15 chunk) sends `PUT /api/profile { jam: { code, hostToken } }` 2 s after the session starts and every 5 minutes while hosting (`JAM_EVERY_MS`), and `jam: null` when the session ends (`hostTokenFor(code)` in `services/together`). The Worker checks the host token against the room (`safeEqual`, else `403`), refuses a room its host has not updated in 3 minutes (`409 ended`, also when the room goes between the check and the write) and stores `profiles.jam_code`; with the switch off no code is stored and turning it off clears one; a write that only touches `jam` keeps `updated_at`, so an old now-playing never looks fresh. Who sees the code (`jamFor`, `liveRoomCodes` in `_lib/social.ts`): `GET /api/follow` and `GET /api/profile/<handle>` carry `jamCode` only between mutual followers, and only while `rooms` says the host updated the room in the last 3 minutes (`JAM_LIVE_MS`; a failed check sends no code); signed-out visitors, one-way followers and the owner never get it; people in a jam sort first in the following list. The column references `rooms (code) on delete set null`, so ending the room, the 6-hour sweep or any deleted room clears it and a reused code never leads friends to a stranger's room; `room_sweep()` also clears codes whose room has been idle 15 minutes. Home's **Friends are listening** shows **Join** (a plain link to `/together?code=<code>` from `jamPath()`, no Together engine import) and "Hosting Listen Together" for a friend who hosts but shares no song; `/u/<handle>` shows **Join their room** and the People tab of `/following` a **Join**; all three use `features/social/JoinRoomLink.tsx`, shown only when the server sent a `jamCode`. The app keeps a `jamCode` only if it matches `^[A-Z0-9]{4,8}$`, and never keeps friends' codes in the stored following list (`withoutJams()` in `features/social/store.ts`), so a restart never shows a stale Join.

Host pushes the playing song, position, play state and next eight songs on any change (or a seek over 2 s); polls members/reactions/requests every 4 s. Guest polls every 2 s, heartbeats every third poll. The room only names a song (id, title, artwork; the server keeps no stream URLs), so a guest looks the host's song up in the catalogue (`getSong`) before playing it, as the host does for a guest's request; a song the guest's device cannot get is said once, shown on the room page, and retried after 20 s. `GET /api/room` returns the server clock `now`, so `HostClock` projects the host playhead as `position + (now - updated_at) + half the round trip`. Corrections seek only when drift exceeds 1.0 s playing (2.0 s paused), never while buffering, within 3 s of the last correction, or within 1.5 s of a track start. A guest whose song ran out within 2 s of the host's end waits for the next song instead of pressing play. Only a host `update` moves `updated_at` (a chat line does not), because followers measure the playhead from it. Two failed play attempts raise `needsTap`. No host push for 90 s marks the host away (a note, not a disconnect). Server: `functions/api/room.ts` keeps `rooms` and `room_members`; the six-character code is the join credential; the host token (returned once) authorises `update`/`end`; guest `request` goes through the atomic `room_request` function; rooms idle six hours are swept by housekeeping; without the database every verb answers `503 not_configured`.

### Service worker (`frontend/public/sw.js`)

| Request | Strategy |
| --- | --- |
| Page navigations the app answers | Network first; cached shell offline |
| Hashed `/assets/` | Cache first |
| `/offline-audio/<id>` | From the `vinax-audio-v1` cache with Range support (Android's copies and songs saved in the browser) |
| Catalogue APIs, artwork, audio streams, `/api/*`, `/admin/`, status page | Passed through, never cached |

The build emits `/precache-manifest.json` (`{ v: 2, precache, onDemand, light }`: `precache` = everything reachable from the entry; `onDemand` = chunks nothing reaches; `light` = a subset of `precache` built by `splitPrecache` in `vite.config.ts`: the entry graph through static and dynamic imports, stopping at route page chunks other than `LIGHT_PAGES` (Offline, Library, Now Playing) and at on-demand chunks, with their stylesheets and assets). A `PRECACHE` message downloads the list, refreshes shell entries and prunes assets in neither list; `{ type: 'PRECACHE', mode: 'light' }` downloads only `light` (the full list when the manifest has none) with the same pruning. Install takes the light precache; the page asks for the full one right after unless it is saving data (see [App shell](#app-shell-frontendsrcmaintsx)). The audio cache is never cleared on activate. The worker also shows push notifications and routes their clicks.

### Worker routes (`backend/worker/index.ts`)

Router plus an adapter giving each handler `{ request, env, params, next, waitUntil, data }`. Resolution order: (1) host middleware `functions/_middleware.ts` (apex -> `www` fallback, `update.` -> APK download, `admin.` -> `/admin/`); (2) exact-path table for every `/api/**` handler, `/apk`, `/img`; (3) dynamic entity routes (`/song/:id`, ...) kept in code but not Worker routes in production; (4) `/api/cat/*`, the self-hosted catalogue (stream URLs resolved at play time and redirected, audio bytes do not pass through the Worker; the `/api/cat/stream` 302s carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` so a song saved in the browser, a CORS fetch, can follow them); (5) single-segment hub allow-list `functions/[hub].ts`; (6) anything else fetched from `ASSETS_HOST`. A module with no handler for the method answers `405`. **A new handler under `worker/functions/api/` needs an import and an exact-path entry in `worker/index.ts`**; `worker/__tests__/routerCoverage.test.ts` fails otherwise (an unrouted handler falls through to the static site and answers 405, which a dry-run cannot see).

| Route family | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `/api/cat/*`, `/api/recommendations`, `/api/blocklist` | Catalogue and content; "songs like these" |
| `/api/events`, `/api/feedback`, `/api/geo`, `/api/username`, `/api/room` | Consented usage events, feedback, coarse region, handle (`409 taken` with suggestions), Listen Together |
| `/api/appconfig`, `/api/experiments`, `/api/announcements`, `/api/site-mode`, `/api/version`, `/api/status`, `/api/apk`, `/api/robots` | Published settings (sanitised by `_lib/clientConfig.ts`, edge-cached), experiments, site mode, Android update source, live probes + 90-day history |
| `/api/push/*` | VAPID key; subscribe/register write `push_devices` (channel `web` or `fcm`); unsubscribe; `push/opened` |
| `/api/auth/*`, `/api/account/data` | Google sign-in, signed 30-day session, `accounts` row; synced copy in `account_backups` with optimistic versions (`200`, `204`, `409`) |
| `/api/profile`, `/api/follow`, `/api/shared-playlist` | Public profiles (and a friend's live jam code, mutual followers only), follows, shared playlists |
| `/api/ai/*` | VinaX AI; provider keys never leave the Worker |
| `/api/cron/run` | Run one job now (`x-cron-secret` or owner token) |
| `/api/charts/vinax` | The public "Most played on VinaX this week" chart: `stats_trending(7, 100)` cut to songs at least 5 accounts played, public fields only; cached 3 h (see [Public routes that read or write it](#public-routes-that-read-or-write-it)) |
| `/api/trends` | Always empty (`sources: []`); called only by older installed apps, or when the client config says `trendsConnected` (off today) |
| `/api/trending-searches` | `{ queries: [] }`; the current app no longer calls it (search chips come from the catalogue's top searches); kept for older installed apps |
| `/api/handoff` | Retired (Move to a new device): answers `410 { error: 'gone' }` with CORS to `GET` and `POST` (and the `OPTIONS` preflight) for old APKs; stores and reads nothing; delete it and its `worker/index.ts` entry later |
| `/api/admin/*` | Owner console API, server-side admin auth on every request; `/api/admin/quota` reads today's Worker requests from Cloudflare's analytics and needs no database |

Data layer `_lib/db.ts`: only code that talks to the database; service-role key; 8-second deadline per request; paging above 1,000 rows; results never throw (`{ ok, rows }` or `{ ok: false, error }` with `not_configured | unauthorized | not_found | conflict | bad_request | unavailable`). Companions: `edge.ts`, `identity.ts` (signed device ids), `settings.ts` (`SETTING_KEYS`, one-minute memo), `audit.ts` (secret redaction), `admin.ts`.

### How a play flows

1. Tap a song -> `playQueue()` on the player store runs intake gates (Kid mode drops explicit songs; duplicates collapse), starts a playback instance, loads the audio engine, publishes to the media session.
2. With "Build every queue" on, the store lazily loads the recommendation engine and asks for the next five songs, in the seed's language, familiar first, inside one deadline; songs pass the admission gate and are appended.
3. The playback instance measures what is heard; a play counts toward taste after five seconds heard; an early leave is marked skipped in history and session signals only.
4. Near the end of a song the store asks `crossfadePlan()` (`store/playerGuards.ts`): nothing with crossfade off or at 0 s, outside the last `crossfadeSeconds`, on a song no longer than twice that, on repeat-one, with no next song, before a podcast episode or a song without streams, during Listen Together, while casting or in karaoke; only a fade-out with a sleep timer or A-B loop running; otherwise `audioEngine.overlap(ms)` (ms = time left / speed) and `handleEnded()`, so the song is credited as ended and `next()` starts the incoming one, which fades in over the overlap. A skip keeps the short 1.2 s fade-in.
5. Only changed persisted fields (queue, index) are written.

Data flows: listening events -> `personalization/updater` -> `vinax.profile.v1` (and, with consent, usage analytics); candidates -> hard filter -> scoring -> sequencer -> validation -> admission gate -> queue; owner settings -> `POST /api/admin/settings` -> `settings` row (+ history + audit) -> `/api/appconfig` (sanitised, cached) -> app (5-minute stale time); consented usage -> `POST /api/events` + `x-vinax-consent` -> `events` + `listeners` -> `stats_*` functions -> console; housekeeping purges old events.

## Repository layout

```text
frontend/
  index.html                  shell markup and the pre-paint script (theme, app style)
  src/main.tsx                boot: migrations, global styles, render, service worker
  src/router/                 routes; every page is a lazy chunk
  src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx   the frame: sidebar, top bar, dock, player, overlays
  src/pages/                  routed pages
  src/components/             shared UI
  src/features/               feature folders (home, search, player, radio, together, settings, vinaxai, ...)
  src/store/                  state stores
  src/services/               recommendation, personalization, api, audio, media-session, push, storage, ai, personal, ...
  src/constants/              app styles, version.ts, changelog.ts (What's New card), storage-keys.ts
  src/styles/                 tokens (index.css), frame (shell.css), templates/, pages/
  public/                     sw.js, admin/ (owner console), status/ (status page), fonts/, _headers, _redirects, robots.txt
  scripts/                    prerender, changelog.json, bundle budget, CSP hashes, e2e smoke, edge integrity, Android patch, evaluation, auto-release
  e2e/                        browser specs and harness
  eval/                       offline evaluation of next-song selection
  native-android/, android-res/   native code and resources copied into the generated Android project
  ci/debug-keystore.b64       public CI debug keystore (never signs a release)
backend/
  worker/index.ts             router and scheduled handler
  worker/functions/api/       public, auth/, account, push, cron, ai/, personal/, admin/ endpoints
  worker/functions/_lib/      db.ts and companions, catalogue, admin auth, rate limits, ai/, personal/, trends/
  worker/__tests__/           Worker tests
  worker/wrangler.toml        name, routes, vars, bindings, Cron Trigger
  server/                     the same API as a Node server (no daily request limit): app.ts, bindings.ts, http.ts, build.mjs
  server/deploy/              VM setup script, systemd unit, deploy script
  supabase/schema.sql         the whole database (idempotent)
  supabase/reset.sql          drops everything so schema.sql can start over
  supabase/check.sql, ci.sh   the schema proof run by the Schema workflow
  supabase/remove-ai.sql      one-off removal of the pre-version-3 AI tables
  scripts/ai-probe.mjs        provider probe (npm run ai:probe)
  scripts/cf-edge-rules.mjs   zone WAF rules operator script
  .env.example                every Worker secret/var name, documented
shared/identity-vectors.json  song-identity test vectors run by both packages
sandbox/                      VinaX AI artifact preview runner, its own static site
.github/workflows/            checks, deploys, Android builds, uptime probe, SEO build
```

Do not commit secrets, generated build folders (`dist/`, `frontend/android/`) or personal exports.

## Running locally

Node.js 22 or newer and npm. Start the Worker first, then the app.

```sh
# Terminal 1 - the Worker on port 8787 (wrangler dev)
cd backend && npm ci && npm run dev

# Terminal 2 - the app on port 5173
cd frontend && npm ci && npm run dev
```

Open `http://localhost:5173`. The dev server proxies `/api`, `/img` and `/apk` to `127.0.0.1:8787` (`frontend/vite.config.ts`).

Secrets are optional for a first run: without them the catalogue works and the owner console cannot sign in; without the two database secrets data features answer their "not configured" shapes (no sync, rooms, feedback, push registration) and console pages show setup cards. Local secrets go in `backend/worker/.dev.vars` (git-ignored), one `NAME=value` per line; names are in `backend/.env.example`. Never put a secret in a `VITE_*` variable.

| Where | Command | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `frontend/` | `npm run preview` | Serves the built `dist/` |
| `frontend/` | `npm run format` / `backend/` `npm run format` | Prettier on `src/` / `worker/` |
| `frontend/` | `node scripts/eval-recs.mjs` | Offline evaluation of next-song selection |
| `frontend/` | `npm run cap:add:android`, `npm run cap:sync`, `npm run android:debug` | Generate, sync, build Android locally (Java 21 JDK + Android SDK) |
| `frontend/` | `npm run deploy:pages` | Build and `wrangler pages deploy dist --project-name vinax` |
| `backend/` | `npm run deploy` | `wrangler deploy --config worker/wrangler.toml` |
| `backend/` | `npm run ai:probe` | Provider/voice probe with your own keys |

### Where to change things

| To change | Edit |
| --- | --- |
| Design tokens, frame, page styles | `src/styles/index.css`, `shell.css`, `pages/*.css` |
| An app style | `src/styles/templates/`, `src/constants/templates.ts`, `src/utils/theme.ts`, pre-paint script in `index.html` ([Adding an app style](#adding-an-app-style)) |
| The shell | `src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx`, `components/TopBar.tsx`, `BottomNav.tsx`, `Sidebar.tsx` |
| Routes | `src/router/index.tsx` |
| What plays next | `src/services/recommendation/`, `src/store/playerStore.ts` |
| Welcome sheet, tours, Help | `components/OnboardingSheet.tsx`, `features/tutorials/tutorials.ts`, `components/TutorialRunner.tsx`, `features/help/helpContent.ts`, `pages/HelpPage.tsx`; their Telugu, Hindi, Tamil, Kannada and Malayalam text in `src/i18n/help/` (below) |
| App language (words on screen) | `src/i18n.ts`, `src/i18n/{te,hi,ta,kn,ml}.ts`, `src/i18n/help/{te,hi,ta,kn,ml}.ts` (below) |
| Backup and restore | `features/settings/backup.ts`, `BackupCenter.tsx` |
| The owner console | `public/admin/` |
| Android native code | `native-android/`, `scripts/patch-android.js` |

**App language** (Settings > Appearance > App language: English, Telugu, Hindi, Tamil, Kannada, Malayalam, each named in its own script (ಕನ್ನಡ, മലയാളം); `uiLanguage` in `settingsStore`, default English; nothing reads the device language). On phones (639 px and narrower) the six choices show as two rows of three: `Segmented` in `features/settings/SettingsControls.tsx` takes an optional `cols`, which makes it a grid with `--seg-cols` columns there, its thumb placed and sized from the chosen button, and each button's tap area kept inside half the 2px row gap (`segmented.test.ts`, `segmentedGrid.test.tsx`); without `cols` it is the usual one row. The keys are the English strings, so a string with no translation shows in English. `src/i18n.ts` has two layers: about 30 sidebar and greeting words per language sit in the first load (`CORE`), so the menu is never English for a beat; everything else is in `src/i18n/te.ts`, `hi.ts`, `ta.ts`, `kn.ts` and `ml.ts` (531 keys each), one lazy chunk per language fetched once the app language asks for it (`ensureDictionary`, also called by `initI18n` on boot); until it lands those strings show in English and `useT` re-renders. They cover Settings, the song menu, section and page titles, the player bar and mini player (every button label, the sleep chip), Now Playing (tabs, Playing from, credits, the Playback sheet, Tune this queue chips, its toasts), Search (placeholder, tabs, filters, sort, moment tiles, mood chips, Recent searches, the result count, every empty and no-results state), the welcome sheet and its tour slides, the sign-in pages (`/login`, `/signup`, `/forgot-password`, the code and password forms, every email sign-in error) and the common toasts. VinaX, VinaX AI and Google stay in English; the email placeholder stays literal.

- **In a component:** `const t = useT();` then `t('Shuffle on')`. `{name}` slots are filled from a second argument: `t('Tune queue: {name}', { name })`; a slot with no value stays as written.
- **Outside React** (stores, services, toasts built in a store): `tr(key, vars)` from `@/i18n` reads the app language at the moment it is called, so use it when the text is made, not at module load.
- **Plurals** are two keys: `'Added 1 song to queue'` and `'Added {n} songs to queue'`.
- **Shared components translate what they are given:** `EmptyState`, `ErrorState` and `InlineError` (`components/States.tsx`), `SectionHeader` (title and explanation) and the snackbars (`Toasts.tsx`, message and action label) run their English text through `useT`, so for a literal caller adding the dictionary key is enough. A toast with numbers or names is translated where it is made, with `tr`.
- **Adding a string:** wrap it, then add the same English key to all five of `src/i18n/te.ts`, `hi.ts`, `ta.ts`, `kn.ts` and `ml.ts`, keeping every `{slot}` exactly as in English. `src/i18n.test.ts` fails when the files differ in keys, when a translation's slots differ from its key's, or when a literal `t(...)` / `tx(...)` / `tr(...)` key in one of the scanned files (the `WRAPPED` list: the player bar, Now Playing, Search, the welcome sheet, the sign-in pages, the shared states and the snackbars) is missing; keys built at run time (tab and sort labels, Tune options, moods, Search moments) are checked from their lists. Add a newly wrapped file to `WRAPPED`. Use short everyday wording. The Help translations quote Settings paths and song-menu items in the words the main dictionaries show, so changing one of those translations may mean updating `src/i18n/help/` too.
- **Help and the tours** have their own lazy dictionaries, separate from the main ones: `src/i18n/help/{te,hi,ta,kn,ml}.ts` (shape and the typed `HELP_UI_KEYS` list of page and tour-card words in `types.ts`), loaded by `features/help/helpI18n.ts` only when Help (`pages/HelpPage.tsx`) or a tour (`components/TutorialRunner.tsx`) opens in that language, once per session, never in the first load. Guides and FAQ entries are keyed by their stable `id` in `helpContent.ts`, tours by tour id with one entry per step; group names and shortcut descriptions by their English text. Only the words are swapped: a tour step's target, route, action and `when` stay the English definition's, so a tour finds the same controls in every language. English shows while the file loads, for anything missing, and for any guide or tour whose step count no longer matches English. Help search matches the translated and the English words. Adding, removing or re-ordering a guide, FAQ entry, guide step, tour step or tip means updating the five files too (`features/help/helpI18n.test.ts` fails until the ids and step counts match); changing English wording alone is not caught by any test, so update the translations by hand. Still English on purpose: the owner's own FAQ from the console, the What's New card, the tutorial's "Could not start a song right now" toast and the prerendered `/help` page for crawlers.
- **Adding an app language** (Kannada and Malayalam were added this way after Telugu, Hindi and Tamil): add it to `UiLang` and `UI_LANGS` in `src/i18n.ts` (the Settings picker reads `UI_LANGS`) and to `uiLanguage`'s type, default and allowed values in `store/settingsStore.ts`; a `CORE` block of the sidebar words (first load, about 0.37 KB gzipped per language; Kannada and Malayalam together added about 0.75 KB and fitted the [bundle budget](#bundle-budget-frontendscriptscheck-bundle-sizemjs); when it no longer fits, re-base the budget with a ledger entry, or keep those words in the lazy file and accept a short English flash); a `LOADERS` entry and `src/i18n/<lang>.ts` with every key; the language in the dictionaries-match test and the native-label and core-words checks of `i18n.test.ts`; a phone-width check of the Settings picker (`cols` on its `Segmented`); optionally `src/i18n/help/<lang>.ts` and its `helpI18n.ts` loader (without one, Help reads in English); and a check that the font stack renders the script well.

## Checks and testing

### The gates

CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) and `e2e.yml` run these on every pull request and push to `main`. Run them locally in this order.

```sh
cd frontend
npm ci
npm run lint                         # eslint src scripts --max-warnings 0
npm run typecheck                    # tsc --noEmit
npm test                             # vitest run
npm run build                        # clean, tsc, vite build, prerender, entity prerender, changelog.json, admin stamp, robots
node scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs   # needs the build
npx playwright-core install chromium # once
npm run e2e                          # needs the build
```

```sh
cd backend
npm ci
npm run lint                         # eslint worker --max-warnings 0
npm run typecheck
npm test
npx wrangler deploy --config worker/wrangler.toml --dry-run --outdir /tmp/wrangler-dry
```

`npm run build` first deletes `dist/` and the build caches (`prebuild:clean`) - do not run it in a checkout whose dev server someone is using; use the worktree recipe below. Schema changes have their own workflow ([Database](#database)); `lighthouse.yml` audits prerendered routes (accessibility and search checks fail the run; performance is advisory).

### Bundle budget (`frontend/scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs`)

Reads `dist/index.html`, sums the gzipped `/assets/*.js` it references. In the script today: first-load JavaScript total `FIRST_LOAD_BUDGET` = **195 KB** gzipped, each first-load chunk under **80 KB**, each on-demand chunk under **160 KB**; a value equal to a limit fails. The script header is the ledger: every time the first-load limit moved it records date, measured sizes and why the code cannot be lazy. Grow it only with an entry in the same style. A few large diagram/maths libraries are exempt only while they stay out of the first load. The `core` chunk group in `vite.config.ts` lists modules by explicit path, never a folder; before adding a path confirm first-load code imports the module, rebuild and re-run the budget.

### Unit tests

Both packages use `vitest`. Frontend tests sit next to code as `*.test.ts(x)` plus `src/__tests__/`; default environment Node, DOM files start with `// @vitest-environment jsdom`; `e2e/`, `dist/`, `android/` are excluded. Backend handler tests sit next to handlers with wider ones in `worker/__tests__/`. `frontend/src/__fixtures__/songs.ts` has deterministic `makeSong` fixtures (no randomness, no clock). Run one file: `npx vitest run <path>`.

### Contract tests (they lock a rule, not a behaviour)

| Test | Guards |
| --- | --- |
| `frontend/src/constants/templates.test.ts` | One app style (`vinax`), the default; unknown and earlier ids fall back to it, no entry names another product; the style file imported by `templates/index.css` and styles only its own selector; colour blocks carry `:root` for their weight in the cascade; accent applies only while no accent chosen; pre-paint canvas equals `--ink-900`; every accent contrast-checked in every style and both themes; Black stays true black and high contrast stays above every style |
| `frontend/src/utils/theme.test.ts`, `src/__tests__/contrast.test.ts` | Colour tokens, one 10/14/20 px radius scale, glass dials, theme resolution (`system` follows OS; `auto` is light 07:00-18:59), WCAG AA token pairs |
| `src/features/settings/TemplatePicker.test.tsx` | Picker is one radio group; arrows move/wrap; cross-fade via view transition unless reduced motion |
| `src/features/tutorials/tutorials.test.ts` + runner/onboarding tests | Every tour target (`data-tour`, aria-label, id, class) exists in source |
| `src/i18n.test.ts` | English is the key and the fallback; `te`, `hi`, `ta`, `kn` and `ml` have the same keys, none empty, and each translation keeps exactly its key's `{slots}`; every literal key in the scanned screens (`WRAPPED`) and every run-time list they translate is in all five; `UI_LANGS` and their native labels; each lazy dictionary loads and fills slots; `document.documentElement.lang` follows the app language; `pickSettings` keeps every app language and drops an unknown one |
| `src/features/help/helpI18n.test.ts` | Every guide, FAQ entry and tour has a unique id; each Help translation has every guide and tour step for step (a tip where English has one), every group, question, shortcut and `HELP_UI_KEYS` word with the same placeholders, and nothing English no longer has; VinaX, Listen Together and Flow stay in English |
| `src/features/help/helpContent.test.ts` | Every "Settings → A → B" path in the Help guides and FAQ names a section, group or row `SettingsPage.tsx` (and the modules it imports) still has; the theme guide lists every Theme option; removed things (Black, Auto, accent, Dynamic theme, plum/amber, Move to a new device, handoff) are never named; the account FAQ covers Google, email + password and browsing first |
| `src/__tests__/cspHashes.test.ts` | Inline-script hashes in `public/_headers` match `index.html` (after changing an inline script: build, then `node scripts/csp-hashes.mjs`) |
