The + menu's **Create image** (shown only when `models.images.enabled`) runs `@cf/black-forest-labs/flux-1-schnell` on the `AI` binding at 4 steps (`{ prompt, steps: 4 }`). It shares the account's free ~10,000 neurons/day Workers AI pool with the picked-by-hand chat models; an image is counted as 60 neurons (4 tiles x 4.8 + 4 steps x 9.6 = 57.6, rounded up on purpose). Caps, both taken through `ai_take_daily` before the model runs and not given back when it fails: per person `img:<account>` (`vinax-ai.images.perDay`, default 10, 1-50) and everyone together `images` (50 a day, about 3,000 neurons); a counter that cannot be read refuses. No schema change. Order of checks: per-address rate limit `ai-images` (5 a minute), session, database, `aiOpenFor`, the `images` switch (`503 images_off`), the binding, then conversation ownership, parent and active leaf, `MAX_MESSAGES`, `filesPerChat` and project ownership for a new chat. Prompt checks: control characters removed, `400 empty_prompt`, `413 prompt_too_long` over 1,000 characters (`MAX_PROMPT_CHARS`), and a small word list refuses sexual prompts and prompts pairing children with violence (`400 prompt_refused`). The answer may be `{ image: base64 }`, raw bytes or a stream; PNG, JPEG (FLUX's usual) or WebP is read from the first bytes. Nothing is created until the image exists; then the chat is made if needed (title from the prompt, incognito and project respected), the picture is stored in the private `ai-attachments` bucket at `<account>/<conversation>/<id>` with an `ai_attachments` row (so chat and account deletion remove it), and two turns are saved: "Create an image: <prompt>" and "Here’s your image." with the picture as an attachment part (`model_id` `cloudflare:@cf/black-forest-labs/flux-1-schnell`, labelled "FLUX.1 schnell · Cloudflare"). Other answers: `429 image_daily_limit | images_busy | rate_limited` (a spent pool or a provider rate limit is `images_busy`, "try tomorrow"), `422 image_refused`, `502 image_failed`, `503 ai_disabled | ai_not_configured | unavailable`, plus the chat's `401 signed_out`, `404 not_found`, `409 conversation_full | too_many_files`. Logs never carry the prompt. Later chat replies see only an "[image: name]" mention of the picture (materialize sends images from user turns only). The page (all in the lazy `/vinaxai` chunk): image mode puts an **Image** pill (× leaves) in the composer, the placeholder "Describe the image you want", no Live voice button and Send labelled **Create image**; `store.createImage()` shows the turn at once with "Creating image…" and swaps in the saved turn (`image-done`); a refusal removes the turn, puts the text back and shows a plain notice; Stop works. The reply shows the picture large (`MadeImages` in `FileChips.tsx`) with **Open full size** and **Download**; Copy, Read aloud and Retry are hidden on it, and editing the "Create an image: …" question sends it to the chat model as text. Not tested against the real model yet.

### Files (`_lib/ai/attachments.ts`, `materialize.ts`, `storage.ts`, `geminiFiles.ts`; page `files.ts`, `pdf.ts`, `usePendingFiles.ts`, `FileChips.tsx`)

From the + menu, paste or drop. Images (png, jpg, webp, gif first frame): 10 per message, shrunk on the device to 1568 px (WebP, JPEG fallback), server takes <= 5 MB. PDF: 30 MB and 500 pages (owner up to 50 MB / 1,000 pages; the bucket takes 50 MB). Text/markdown/csv/json/source: 2 MB (owner up to 5). 20 files per message, 50 per chat; the page uses the `limits` from `GET /api/ai/models`. Upload is immediate: `POST /api/ai/attachments?name=...&conversationId=...` (raw bytes; type, length and first bytes checked) streams into the **private** bucket `ai-attachments` at `<account>/<conversation or new>/<id>` without buffering; a text file's text is kept; a PDF's page count and text are read on the device by pdf.js (loaded only when a PDF is added; worker ~370 KB gzipped, an `.mjs` asset outside the bundle budget's `.js` check) and sent with `PATCH`. `GET .../attachments/:id` returns the owner's file with its own type, `nosniff`, a sandboxing CSP, `inline` for images only; no public or signed URL is ever made. Sending: vision models get image parts from the last three questions only (older become "[image: name]"; a model that cannot see gets a note; the composer warns before sending; the server refuses with `400 model_cannot_see_images`); Gemini reads PDFs through the **Files API** (the Worker streams from Storage; saved URI re-uploaded an hour before Google's 48 hours expire); other models get the device-read text as `<document name="...">` (`</document>` inside is defused). Auto sends images to the vision ladder and PDFs to the long ladder. Deleting: with the account, the chat, or after a day if never sent (a project's files are kept until removed).

### Artifacts and the sandbox

Since 2026-11-16 the prompt asks for fenced blocks instead: a ` ```html ` page (one complete, self-contained file), ` ```svg ` and ` ```mermaid `, plus ` ```questions ` (up to 3 clarifying questions as JSON `[{question, options}]`) and ` ```quiz ` (`[{question, options, answer, explanation}]`). A finished ` ```html ` block that is a page (a doctype, `<html>`, `<head>`, `<body>`, `<script>`, `<style>` or `<canvas>`, or 15+ lines; a short snippet stays code), and every ` ```svg ` / ` ```mermaid ` block, is an artifact (`splitFenced` in both parsers): its title and id come from the html/svg `<title>` or Mermaid front-matter `title:` (else Page / Illustration / Diagram), so the same title in a later reply is a new version; an unclosed block is an incomplete artifact while it streams. `mayHaveArtifacts` gates the parsing. Questions and quizzes render as cards (`ChoiceBlocks.tsx`): on the latest reply a questions card sends the picked or typed answers as the next message (one question: a tap sends it); a quiz plays one question at a time with the right answer, the explanation and a score at the end; JSON that doesn't parse shows as code. Older replies may hold `<artifact id="..." type="code|html|svg|mermaid|markdown|react" title="..." language="...">...</artifact>` tags, still parsed (same id again to update). The tolerant parser exists in the Worker (`_lib/ai/`) and the page (`features/vinaxai/`): attributes in any order, unknown type reads as code, missing id from title, an unclosed tag closes at reply end marked incomplete, a tag inside a code fence is code. Saved in `ai_messages.artifacts`. On the page: cards in the reply, an artifacts panel (Preview/Code, Copy, Download, versions "v2 of 3", resizable 40-60% on wide screens, full-screen sheet on narrow), live previews under finished html/svg/mermaid/react artifacts (440 px, 360 for diagrams, 320 on phones; Full screen and Open buttons in the corner, mounted within 300 px of the screen; Settings > General > Live previews), and **Fix it** after a failed preview (sends "The diagram "<title>" didn't work. The preview said: <error> Please fix it and send the complete corrected artifact."). Mermaid labels that fail to parse are quoted by the runner (`quoteLabels` in `sandbox/index.html`) and retried once. HTML previews get `THREE_HD` before the page's scripts: the first time the page reads `THREE`, its `WebGLRenderer` is wrapped to be antialiased, prefer the fast GPU and draw at the screen's pixel ratio (up to 2) whatever the page asked for. The panel's Code view scrolls inside the code box both ways (line numbers stay on the left).

**The sandbox runner** (`sandbox/index.html`, `https://sandbox.vinaxmusic.online/`): framed with `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-modals"` and no `allow-same-origin` (opaque origin: no storage, cookies or session); `postMessage` `{ type: 'render', kind, content, theme }`; listens only to `https://www.vinaxmusic.online` (and localhost) and answers `ready|rendered|error`; CSP (`sandbox/_headers`): `default-src 'none'`, scripts only from cdnjs and two pinned jsDelivr paths (`fixScripts`: an HTML page's `<script src>` for the main file of Chart.js, d3, Plotly, three, lodash, mathjs, PapaParse, Mermaid, React or Babel on jsDelivr or unpkg is pointed at the same library on cdnjs, never an add-on path (`examples/`, `addons/`, `jsm/`, `src/`); a three.js build cdnjs only has under its number (`r152`) becomes `0.152.0`; OrbitControls from anywhere is put inline from three.js r128's copy kept in the page (`#vx-orbit-controls`, MIT), since cdnjs has no three.js add-ons. Seen 2026-11-12: a Neon Metropolis scene whose OrbitControls address had been pointed at three.min.js stayed black), `connect-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors https://www.vinaxmusic.online http://localhost:*`. Renders HTML in a nested sandboxed frame, with a reporter sent first (after `<head>`) whose script errors, failed loads and blocked loads come back as `error` (so the panel shows them and offers Fix it instead of a blank page), SVG as an `<img>` from a blob, Mermaid 11.12.0 (`securityLevel: 'strict'`), React compiled by Babel standalone 7.26.10 and mounted with React 18.3.1 with hooks, Recharts 2.15.3, Tailwind in-browser 4.3.3 and lucide 1.57.0 (shim for `lucide-react`), and, loaded only when imported, d3 7.9.0, three 0.149.0, Chart.js 4.4.1 (with a `react-chartjs-2` shim), Plotly 2.34.0 (with a `react-plotly.js` shim), lodash 4.17.21, mathjs 13.2.0, PapaParse 5.4.1, and Tailwind versions of the common shadcn/ui components (`@/components/ui/*`, also `@shadcn/ui` and `shadcn/ui`: card, button, input, textarea, label, badge, separator, alert, tabs, progress, slider, switch, checkbox, table, and a `Typography` that models often import); any other import fails clearly, naming what is available. If the runner is not ready within 8 s the panel says "Preview needs the sandbox host" (Code/Copy/Download still work). **Open in new tab** (panel header, html/svg/mermaid/react) opens the runner at the sandbox origin in a tab of its own (`openInNewTab`, `RunnerFrame.tsx`): it says `ready` to the VinaX tab that opened it, gets the artifact, cuts `window.opener` and runs it full size, titled with the artifact's name. While an artifact is written its code shows as plain current text (highlighting waits for a pause) and the panel follows the newest line unless you scroll up.

**Artifacts gallery** `/vinaxai/artifacts` (`ArtifactsGallery.tsx`): one card per artifact id per chat (newest version, kind, version count, chat title, day), kind chips, title search, infinite scroll; opens the same panel; **Open chat** goes to `/vinaxai/c/<id>?m=<reply>&a=<artifact>`. Nothing new stored: `ai_artifact_list` unpacks `ai_messages.artifacts` for non-deleted chats (metadata only).

### Voice (`_lib/ai/voice.ts`, `voiceGate.ts`; routes `transcribe.ts`, `speak.ts`, `live-token.ts`, `live-transcript.ts`; page `mic.ts`, `readAloud.ts`, `live/`)

Every voice route: per-address bucket, session, account `RATE_LIMIT_10` tier, a key for that feature, the owner's switch; failures answer `voice_busy` (429) or `voice_unavailable` (503), never a provider's words; each failure rests that voice model. Facts that differ from the original brief (docs won, 2026-10-31): Orpheus takes <= **200 characters** a request, WAV only, small free allowance (~10 requests/min, 100/day, 1,200 tokens/min); Gemini TTS names the voice `speechConfig.voiceConfig.voice` (older models `prebuiltVoiceConfig.voiceName`; a 400 retries with the second); ephemeral tokens and the Live socket are **v1beta**; `gemini-2.5-flash-native-audio-latest` works on the owner's probe alongside the dated `-preview-12-2025` and `-preview-09-2025`; `gemini-3.5-transcribe` returns empty transcripts for some users, so the clip is uploaded through the Files API and an empty answer is retried on `gemini-3.8-flash`.

- **Dictation** `POST /api/ai/transcribe`: composer mic records with `MediaRecorder` (WebM/Opus or MP4), <= 2 minutes; raw bytes body; type, size (25 MB) and first bytes checked; tries Groq `whisper-large-v3-turbo`, Groq `whisper-large-v3`, then Gemini (Files API upload; `gemini-3.5-transcribe`, then `gemini-3.8-flash`); answer `{ text }` placed in the composer and **never auto-sent**; nothing stored.
- **Read aloud** `POST /api/ai/speak` `{ text, voice, liveVoice }`: `speechText.ts` strips code blocks (one line), citation markers, links, table pipes, maths, artifacts (named, not read); <= 6,000 characters. Orpheus (`canopylabs/orpheus-v1-english`, default voice Troy; Arabic text `canopylabs/orpheus-arabic-saudi`/Abdullah) for replies <= 800 characters; Gemini TTS (`gemini-3.8-flash-tts`, `-flash-lite-tts`, `gemini-2.5-flash-preview-tts`) in 600-character pieces for longer ones; if Orpheus fails part-way the rest goes to Gemini. **Indian languages** (since 2026-11-17): text mostly in an Indian script (`isMostlyIndic` in `voice.ts`: letters and vowel signs of Devanagari, Bengali, Gurmukhi, Gujarati, Oriya, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada or Malayalam are more than half of its letters; digits and dandas do not count) always goes to Gemini TTS, whatever its length (the first free model of `geminiTtsModels`; a failure part-way moves only to the next Gemini model), and never to Orpheus English. With no Gemini TTS model free (no key, or all resting) the route answers `503 language_unavailable` and the page says "Can’t read this language aloud right now. Try again later." (`voiceNotice` in `api.ts`). Arabic still goes to Orpheus Arabic, Latin text as before. Streamed frames `[kind 1 byte][length 4 bytes][payload]` (1 audio file, 2 base64 Gemini audio, 3 `{ error }`); at most 40 upstream calls (free plan allows 50 subrequests); music ducks to 20% volume.
- **Live voice** `POST /api/ai/live-token` + `POST /api/ai/live-transcript`: the waveform button opens a full-screen view (music pauses). The page opens the mic and in parallel gets a token and connects. Token body `{ thinking, skip, voice, search, language, recap?, context?, personal?, conversationId?, incognito?, lean?, model? }` -> `{ token, url, setup, model, search, tuned }`. Models tried in order: `gemini-3.8-live`, `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`, `gemini-2.5-flash-native-audio-latest`, `-preview-12-2025`, `-preview-09-2025` (Extended thinking: `gemini-3.8-live-extended-thinking` first; a model picked in Settings goes first). Token via `POST /v1beta/auth_tokens` (one use, 2 min to start, 30 min to live), locked to the setup where Google allows (tries `liveConnectConstraints`, then `bidiGenerateContentSetup`, then unlocked; the last accepted body is remembered per isolate). Setup carries `VOICE_PROMPT` (short, spoken, Claude-call style), one of Google's 30 voices (`LIVE_VOICES`), input/output transcription and sliding-window compression, then owner instructions, the chat's project instructions (a call opened on a chat in a project, since 2026-11-17: `liveProject` in `live-token.ts` finds the account's chat and calls `loadProjectContext(…, { files: false })`, so no knowledge text is read; `liveProjectBlock` cuts the instructions to `LIVE_PROJECT_CHARS` 2,000 at a word, marked "[…]", and adds a line saying the project's files are not in a Live call and the typed chat can use them; any error means no project block), personalisation, memory (read-only: a call does not learn; none in incognito), the typed chat so far (`context`, latest 12 turns, as `<this_chat_so_far>`), then `recap` (latest 20 turns, <= 500 characters each / 4,000 total, as `<earlier_in_this_call>`). **Language** (`LIVE_LANGUAGES` or `auto`): native-audio models choose language themselves, so `languageRule` pins it in the prompt (first line) and `languageReminder` repeats it last; transcription gets `languageCodes` hints (`transcriptionLanguages`). **Speed** (`tuned`): `automaticActivityDetection` ends the turn after 500 ms of silence (`END_SENSITIVITY_HIGH`); minimal thinking (`thinkingLevel: "minimal"` on 3.1 Flash Live; `thinkingBudget: 0` on 2.5 Native Audio; none for 3.8 Live); a 1007 close before `setupComplete` while tuned retries the same model `lean: true`. Mic batches 2,048 samples (~43 ms at 48 kHz) through an AudioWorklet (`micTap.worklet.js`, same-origin because the CSP has no `blob:`/`data:` scripts; never inlined: `assetsInlineLimit`), resampled to 16 kHz PCM; Gemini's 24 kHz PCM plays as it arrives (40 ms lead web, 120 ms app); talking over it or tapping the orb stops it (`interrupted`). Tapping to cut in drops only a reply still arriving (one that had fully arrived used to swallow the start of the next). **Models that won't start:** one Gemini refuses before `setupComplete` (a close code, not a timeout, and not with search on) is remembered on the device for 12 hours (`live/noStart.ts`, localStorage `vinax.live.noStart.v1`) and sent as `skip` when Live opens; a model that starts is forgotten; if the skips leave nothing, every model is tried again. **Spoken style** (`VOICE_PROMPT`): contractions, one idea at a time (the count first for several items), tone that follows the moment, "sure" and a wait for "hold on", no repeating the question, and no answer to speech plainly not meant for it (a TV, people talking to each other). `EchoGuard` (`liveProtocol.ts`) mutes mic batches while the voice plays (+250 ms) unless RMS >= 0.025 (-32 dBFS); `NoiseGate` follows the noise floor and sends silence unless 12 dB above it (and above -60 dBFS), holding 350 ms. Search in Live (`search: true` and the owner's switch): setup gets `tools: [{ googleSearch: {} }]` and `VOICE_SEARCH_PROMPT`; Gemini 3.8 Live and 3.1 Flash Live close with a quota error when search is in the setup, so `LIVE_SEARCH_MODELS` (2.5 Native Audio) go first when search is on; sources arrive as `groundingMetadata` (http(s) only, <= 10). The globe button reconnects with a `recap`. Sessions end after ~10 minutes (`goAway`); unexpected closes after `setupComplete` reconnect (<= 2 per session); setup timeout 12 s web / 20 s app. When the view closes the transcript is saved with `POST /api/ai/live-transcript` (`{ conversationId, parentMessageId?, model, turns: [{ role, text, sources? }] }`) as ordinary messages (model id `gemini-live:<model>`, labelled with the Live model); closing the page mid-call (`pagehide` only) sends the transcript so far to the same route with `fetch(…, { keepalive: true })` (`transcriptKeepaliveBody` in `keepOnLeave.ts`: each turn cut to 8,000 characters, the call's first turns kept as far as they fit under about 60 KB, at most the 200 turns the route takes), and End after a back/forward-cache restore does not save it twice; sending the call to the background neither ends nor saves it. Audio is never stored. **In the background** (like ChatGPT's and Claude's voice mode): the arrow at the top of the Live view sends the call to a small bar above the player (`live/liveCall.ts` app-wide store; `live/LiveHost.tsx`, mounted by `AppLayout` while a call is open, lazy), and it keeps going while the listener browses VinaX; the bar shows the orb, the status, mute and End, and opens the full view again. When it ends, the saved chat is refreshed, and opened only if the listener is in VinaX AI. **Music control:** the page asks `music: true`; the setup carries `LIVE_MUSIC_TOOLS` (`play_music { query }`, `pause_music`, `resume_music`, `next_song`, `previous_song`, `now_playing`; a lean setup drops them) and the prompt's `VOICE_MUSIC` replaces "can't play the music"; Gemini's `toolCall` is run by `live/musicTools.ts` in VinaX's player (song search, `playQueue`, toggle, next, prev) and answered with a `toolResponse` (not when Gemini cancelled it; 8 s at most). While music plays the mic is muted ("Muted while music plays") so the song isn't taken for speech; unmuting pauses the music; when the music stops, the page's own mute is lifted. **Showing things:** camera (front/back flip), screen sharing (desktop browsers only) and a photo (`live/liveVideo.ts`): frames go to Gemini as `realtimeInput.video` JPEGs, at most 768 px, about one a second while shared (a photo is one frame), with a preview under the orb; nothing is stored, and sharing stops when switched off or the call ends; `VOICE_SEEING` tells the model it sees only what is shared. The Android app already declares CAMERA. **Call summary** (`_lib/ai/callSummary.ts`): a call with at least two questions and 300 characters said gets two to five bullet points (what was asked, the main answers, any plan or follow-up; only what was said, in the call's language) from the `title` ladder (Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite first; 4 s to start, 7 s in all, written while the call is saved), saved as one more VinaX AI message after the call, headed **Call summary** and labelled with the model that wrote it; the response says `summary: true`. A failure or a slow model means no summary; the call is saved either way.
- **The microphone** (`mic.ts`): the picked mic (`micId`) else the browser's; when the browser's mic is virtual (BlackHole, Soundflower, Loopback, VB-Audio, Voicemeeter, RemotePC, Zoom/Teams drivers...) a real one is opened (`pickRealMic`); a stream of exact zeros means the wrong device ("No sound from your mic" after 1.8 s in dictation, 2.5 s in Live, 6 s in the app); meters map RMS to 0-1 on a -58..-12 dB scale; audio contexts are created inside the tap (Safari); Firefox needs `micSource` at the mic's rate. An APK older than 1.3.10 lacks `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS`: dictation falls back to the phone's recogniser (`createSttSession`), Live voice asks to allow the mic or update.

### Live voice in the Android app

The web view puts the app in `MODE_IN_COMMUNICATION` when a page opens a mic with echo cancellation and the phone has a platform echo canceller; on Android 12+ Chromium picks no output device, so call audio defaults to the **earpiece**; `setSinkId` does nothing in the web view; an audio context made before the mic opened plays as media, outside the echo canceller. Fix: `AudioRoutePlugin.java` (`VinaxAudioRoute`, registered in `MainActivity` by the patch script): `start({ speaker })` sets call mode and (API 31+) `setCommunicationDevice` with the built-in speaker or earpiece from `getAvailableCommunicationDevices()` (older: `setSpeakerphoneOn`); a connected wired/USB/Bluetooth headset is left to Android; `setSpeaker({ on })`, `getRoute()` -> `{ route: speaker|earpiece|headset|other }`, `stop()` (clears the device, restores mode and volume keys; also on bridge destroy). `live/audioRoute.ts` wraps it (no-ops on web and older APKs); `LiveSession` opens the mic first, starts the route (loudspeaker unless the listener last chose the earpiece; `liveSpeaker` pref), then makes the voice's audio context. A loudspeaker toggle shows only when the plugin answered. Over-the-air changes reach every app; the route and button need the new APK. Not verified on a phone: the routing itself, the echo-guard threshold, Android < 12 behaviour, vendor audio stacks; Android blocks the mic for backgrounded apps, so Live hears nothing off screen.

### Settings (`SettingsPanel.tsx`, kept on the device under `vinax.vxai.prefs.v1` except Appearance and Memory)

General (Appearance, Artifacts on/off, Live previews), Personalisation (name, about, reply instructions, style), Memory (Use memory, Learn from my chats, every note, Add a memory, Forget everything), Chat (model, Thinking: Auto/Fast/Standard/Extended/Maximum, Web search), Voice (microphone with a ten-second test, Read aloud voice from Orpheus's English voices, Live voice from ten of Gemini's voices, Live model, Live language, Search the web in Live voice), Your chats (Export all chats, Your artifacts, Shared links).

### Sharing

A chat is shared as a snapshot. `POST /api/ai/shares { conversationId }` stores the active branch (role, text with artifacts, cited sources (http(s), <= 20), time; attachments as `{ name, kind }` only - never id, bytes or text; no reasoning, model, usage or account info; empty replies skipped; > 1 MB -> `413 too_large`; empty -> `422 empty`); one link per chat (`ai_shares.conversation_id` unique; re-snapshot keeps the id; 201 create, 200 update). The id is 22 base64url characters of 128 random bits (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{22}$`). `GET /api/ai/shares/:id` needs no session, `cache-control: public, max-age=60`, `x-robots-tag: noindex, nofollow` (the page `/vinaxai/share/*` is `noindex, nofollow` too; old `/ai/shared/*` 301s to Home via `RETIRED_AI_PATH`). Ending: owner `DELETE`, deleting the chat (link deleted right after hiding), or deleting the account (`on delete cascade`). `POST /api/ai/shares/:id/continue` copies the snapshot into a new chat of the signed-in reader. Rate limits per address: list 30/min, create/update/delete 10/min, read 60/min, continue 5/min. Page: `ShareDialog.tsx` (Create link, Copy link, Share..., Update link, Stop sharing) and `SharedChatView.tsx` (`pages/VinaXAISharePage.tsx`, no composer; signed in -> Continue this chat; Report this chat emails the owner).

### Projects

A project (`_lib/ai/projects.ts`; routes `api/ai/projects.ts`, `projects/[id].ts`; page `Projects.tsx`, `projectDialogs.tsx`): name <= 60, description <= 300, **instructions** <= 8,000 (every chat in the project follows them), **files** (knowledge) <= 20 files and 200,000 characters of text (`409 too_many_files | knowledge_full`). A project file is an `ai_attachments` row with `project_id` and no conversation, stored at `<account>/project-<project id>/<id>`, uploaded with `&projectId=...` (a project and a chat at once is `400`); it can never be a message attachment. `POST /api/ai/chat` takes `projectId` for a new conversation (404 if not the account's); `PATCH conversations/:id { projectId }` moves a chat. The Worker loads the project once per reply (`loadProjectContext`) and per model (`knowledgeFor`): text and PDFs-for-non-PDF-models become `<document>` blocks inside `<project_knowledge>`; a PDF goes to Gemini as a Files API file; up to 5 images go to vision models; the system prompt may take up to half the window (`KNOWLEDGE_SHARE`); Auto sends a chat whose project has a PDF or > 12,000 characters of text to the long ladder (`longKnowledge`). Instructions and files are never stored in a message, never logged and never in a shared link. A Live voice call opened on a chat in a project follows the project's instructions (the first 2,000 characters) but never reads its knowledge files; the project page's hints say so ("Live voice follows the first 2,000 characters.", "Live voice doesn’t read them."). Delete project removes instructions and files; chats stay (`on delete set null`) unless `?chats=delete`. Max 100 projects (`409 too_many_projects`); rate limits 30/min list/read/write per address. Pages `/vinaxai/projects`, `/vinaxai/project/:projectId`; chat header shows the project; sidebar menu "Move to project...".

### Memory

Up to 50 notes of <= 200 characters per account (`ai_memories`, origin `chat` or `user`; switches in `ai_memory_prefs`; no row = both on). `memoryFor` reads the owner's switch, the listener's switches, the chat's `incognito` flag and the notes in parallel with the account lookup; notes go into `<memory>` (marked as possibly out of date, never instructions). A failed read means no memory and no learning for that reply. **Learning** runs after a finished reply (`end|length`), inside the reply's `waitUntil` job: on every third message of the branch (`LEARN_EVERY`) and on messages that ask to remember/forget ("remember...", "forget...", "keep in mind...", "note that..."), new messages only. A small fast model (`title` ladder, then `music` ladder, via `askJson`, 15 s) gets `LEARN_PROMPT` and only `{ memory: [{ id, text }], recent: [last <= 3 user messages (<= 2,000 chars) with replies between (<= 1,500), artifacts -> [artifact: title], code -> [code]] }` and answers `{ add, remove }`. `parseLearnAnswer` enforces: removes name an own note by id or exact text (<= 10); adds are plain text, <= 200 characters, deduplicated, rejected by `looksSensitive` (email, 10+ digits, 6 digits in a row, key-like strings, words like password/PIN/OTP/token/CVV/IBAN/account or card number/Aadhaar/PAN); <= 5 a run, never past 50; the model is told never to keep secrets, contact or financial details, health, sex life, religion, politics, caste/ethnicity or anything about other people. **Incognito chat** (header hat-and-glasses on a new empty chat) neither reads nor writes memory; `ai_conversations.incognito` is read on its own query. Owner: `memory: false` (or `{ enabled: false }`) in `vinax-ai` turns it off for everyone (`503 memory_off`; JSON-only for now). Shared links and Live voice never use memory; deleting a chat keeps its learned notes. Rate limits: reads 30/min, changes 20/min.

### The owner's switches (`vinax-ai` setting)

```json
{ "enabled": false, "testers": ["<account id>"], "search": true,
  "models": { "nvidia:nvidia/nemotron-3.5-bolt-e4b": { "enabled": false }, "gemini:gemini-3.6-flash": { "order": 0 } },
  "instructions": "Suggest a VinaX playlist when music comes up.",
  "dailyLimit": 50, "limits": { "filesPerChat": 50, "pdfPages": 500, "searchesPerDay": null }, "memory": true,
  "images": { "enabled": false, "perDay": 10 } }
```

`enabled` is the kill switch (missing or anything but `true` = off: `503 ai_disabled`, "VinaX AI is taking a break"); `testers` are account ids (<= 50) who may use it while off; `models` switch a model off everywhere or reorder it inside its group (lower `order` first); `search: false` switches web search off for everyone; an old `discover` key (model discovery, removed 2026-11-17) is ignored; `instructions` <= 4,000 characters; `dailyLimit` messages per person per UTC day (`ai_take_daily('msg:<account>', limit)` -> `429 daily_limit`); `images` switches Create image on (off by default; `true` = on with 10 a day) and sets `perDay` (1-50, default 10) images per person, under the fixed 50 a day for everyone (console > Features & AI > VinaX AI > **Images**: a Create image switch, an "Images a day (per person)" slider and a note on the neuron pool); `limits` (clamped by `LIMIT_RANGES` in `config.ts`):

| Key | Range | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `messagesPerMinute` | 1-30 | 20 (per address, burst 2x; each account also has a fixed 30/min) |
| `imagesPerMessage` | 1-20 | 10 |
| `filesPerMessage` | 1-20 | 20 |
| `filesPerChat` | 1-100 | 50 |
| `pdfMB` | 1-50 | 30 |
| `pdfPages` | 1-1,000 | 500 |
| `textMB` | 1-5 | 2 |
| `searchesPerDay` | 0-2,000 or none | none (= `SEARCH_DAILY_LIMIT`, else 100; 0 switches the search API off; free grounding keeps its own 450/day) |

The setting key is `vinax-ai` (the settings vocabulary only has kebab-case keys). Memoised a minute per isolate.

### The console's providers card

Console > App Health > AI Assistant (`GET /api/admin/ai`, owner only): on/off and tester count; each provider's key set/missing (never the value), whether the whole provider rests and its last error; every registry model with group, owner switch, ready/resting (why, until when)/no key; today's grounded searches and search-API calls against daily limits (`ai_daily_usage`); which voice features have keys and which voice models rest; utility models read-only; the **Thumbs up** column on the models table (`feedback: { days: 30, models: { "<model id>": { up, down } } } | null`, [Thumbs up and down](#thumbs-up-and-down-_libaifeedbackts-route-apiaifeedbackts-page-feedbacktsx)); the **Web search** card (search service, searches today against the limit, which follows the owner's searches a day like the chat, and the **Test search** button, see [Web search](#web-search-_libaisearchts-wired-in-chatts)). The Models card says a model joins only through `APPROVED_MODELS` in `models.ts`; there is no found-models card since model discovery was removed. Rests and last errors live in each instance's memory, so the card shows what the instance that answered has seen. **Probe** (`POST /api/admin/ai { model }`, one at a time, curated ids only, approved or paused) sends "Reply with OK" and shows time to first word or the failure (a failure rests the model; an answer clears the rest); spends a little free allowance, only on button press.

### The page

Placement: inside `SiteGate > AuthGate > AppLayout`; AppLayout hides side nav, top bar, rails and player dock on `/vinaxai*`; music keeps playing (now-playing pill, `NowPlayingPill.tsx`; its spark button **Ask about this song** fills the composer with "Tell me about <title> by <artist>" (up to two artists, else the subtitle, else the title alone; appended after a space to a draft) and focuses it, never sending, like the starters; it exists only inside `/vinaxai`, and nothing about the song reaches the model unless the listener sends that text); signed-out visitors go to /login with a line about VinaX AI. Layout: >= 1024 px a 260 px collapsible sidebar (`Sidebar.tsx`: New chat, Projects, Artifacts, search, Starred, Recents grouped Today/Yesterday/Previous 7 days/Older; each row's menu Star, Rename, Move to project..., Delete); narrower a slide-over drawer; time-aware greeting with four starter chips (Write, Learn, Code, Music) that never send; composer stays above on-screen keyboards (`visualViewport`). Look and motion: one calm canvas colour and solid surfaces, no glass and no backdrop blur (reduced transparency also makes the see-through chips and buttons solid; the Live voice mini bar keeps its own blur), all from one stylesheet, `styles/pages/vinaxai.css`, whose last section is the Claude look; skeletons, wave dots then a breathing cursor, only transform/opacity animate and none under reduced motion. Composer: + menu (Web search, Add photos and files, Create image when the owner has it on, Settings), Thinking menu (`ThinkingPicker.tsx`: Auto, Fast, Standard, Extended, Maximum, each with a note when the picked model or Auto would answer at a nearer level), mic dictation, Live voice button where Send is when empty, model picker (`ModelPicker.tsx`: Auto and the groups; capability filter chips All/Recommended/Fast/Reasoning/Code/Vision/Long context, shown only when some model has them; context size per model; badges Vision/PDF/Web/Max thinking/Extended thinking/Thinking/Code; greyed models say why), choices kept in `prefs.ts` (`thinking`; the old `extendedThinking: true` is read as Extended). Under a reply: model · provider, the thinking level when not Standard, Auto's judgement (`Auto: Maths, hard`; this session only, not saved), the time, and a note when a level you chose was not on that model. Code blocks: language, Wrap, Download (`snippet.<ext>` by language), Copy, line numbers from five lines (hidden while wrapped). While a reply starts: "Reading report.pdf…" (the `progress` event), the web search line, then the thinking fold. Messages: Edit makes a sibling (`< 2 / 3 >` switcher saves the active leaf), Retry menu (Auto or every other available model), Read aloud, Good reply / Bad reply ([Thumbs up and down](#thumbs-up-and-down-_libaifeedbackts-route-apiaifeedbackts-page-feedbacktsx)), Continue for cut-short replies, failed picked-model errors offer Retry / Try with Auto / two alternatives. Code: `store.ts` + `chatState.ts` reducer (deltas applied once per animation frame), `Markdown.tsx` (`react-markdown` + `remark-gfm`; raw HTML shown as text; only `http(s)`/`mailto` links; images never loaded), highlight.js core with 22 languages in its own chunk (skipped over 20,000 characters), KaTeX via `remark-math` + `rehype-katex` (`trust: false`, own chunk loaded the first time a reply contains `$`; `mathText.ts` applies Pandoc's rule so prices are not maths). The page, highlighter, maths chunk, Live voice chunk and mic worklet, stylesheets and KaTeX fonts are on demand in the precache (`ON_DEMAND_PAGES`/`ON_DEMAND_ASSETS` in `vite.config.ts`). Entry points: desktop side-nav row (`constants/nav.ts`), profile menu item, command palette; the phone tab bar keeps its four tabs.

## Recommendations on the device

Everything here runs on the device, so a free app can continue a queue sensibly in the listener's languages without sending listening history to a server. Paths relative to `frontend/src/`.

### The pipeline

`planNextSongs(seed, ctx, options)` in `services/recommendation/engine.ts` (`limit` clamped 0-40, default 8; the player asks for 5; `recommendNextSongs` wraps it for radio/playlists/tests; `buildRecommendations(ctx)` runs the same sources, filter and scorer without a seed for Home). The engine is a lazy chunk. Hard rules never relax; a penalty is never a rule. The live queue calls the V2 planner in `services/nextup/` (a warm pool ranked by `rules.ts`, a stretch picked under its spacing rule) with the same context, ledger and admission gate.

| # | Stage | Module (`services/recommendation/`) | What |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Candidate generation | `candidates.ts` | Pool from several sources in parallel; a failed source shrinks the pool |
| 2 | Hard filtering | `filters.ts` | Rules with named rejection reasons; versions of one song collapse |
| 3 | Scoring | `scoring.ts`, `weights.ts`, `vectors.ts` | Every term recorded as a reason; reasons sum to the score |
| 4 | Ranking | `scoring.ts`, `reranking.ts`, `engine.ts` | Sort, shuffle within 0.05 bands by the session salt, greedy diversity re-rank (artist/genre/language vs the last four), active tune nudge |
| 5 | Sequencing | `sequencer.ts` | Top 40 ordered into a stretch: energy arc, mood flow, transition memory, artist/album spacing, era, language policy, familiar first |
| 6 | Validation | `validation.ts` | Re-checks every rule; tops up from the reserve |
| 7 | Admission | `admission.ts` | The player re-checks every automatic queue change against the listener's state at that moment |

What feeds it: taste profile (`services/personalization/profile.ts`, `storage.ts`, `updater.ts`; persisted, decays), event weights (`eventWeights.ts`, `EVENT_WEIGHTS_VERSION` 1.2.0), session window (`session.ts`; `sessionStorage`), session intent (`sessionIntent.ts`; 45 minutes of silence ends a sitting), playback measurement (`services/playback/session.ts`), recommendation context (`context.ts`), exposure ledger and snoozes (`exposure.ts`; `localStorage`), lifetime heard-set (`heardSet.ts`, `bloom.ts`; IndexedDB `vinax-heard`), engine memory (`recMemory.ts`), verified-trend signal (`services/trends/signal.ts`, empty today), queue ownership (`store/playerStore.ts`).

### How listening becomes taste

All learning reads one measurement: seconds actually heard in a playback instance (`services/playback/session.ts`); a run of repeat-one loops of one track learns at most one PLAY, SKIP and COMPLETE; a time update credits a step only when no seek is pending, not buffering, and the step is plausible against the wall clock; playback speed counts.

| Verdict | Duration known | Unknown |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PLAY` | Heard >= the smaller of 5 s and 70% of the song | 5 s |
| `COMPLETE` | Ended naturally with >= 70% heard | Ended naturally after 30 s heard |
| `SKIP` | Manual skip after PLAY counted, < 30% heard | < 30 s heard |
| Early leave | Manual skip before PLAY counted: this sitting's intent only | same |
| Failed | Every source failed: no SKIP, no transition verdict | same |

The player publishes `counted`, `credit`, `end`, `served`, `refined` on one bus (listen clock, transition memory, exposure ledger, opt-in analytics subscribe). **Event weights:** PLAY 1.0, COMPLETE 2.0, FAVORITE 3.0, QUEUE_ADD 0.5, PLAYLIST_ADD 1.0, SEARCH_PLAY 1.5, SKIP -0.75 (and takes back the PLAY: -1.75 total), DISLIKE -1.5, SOFT_MUTE -3.75 ("Less like this" also mutes the lead artist 7/14/30 days, default 14). Positive affinity halves 14 days after the last signal, skips after 30; `MAX_AFFINITY` 60. Passive listening teaches less as a sitting goes on: the first 8 plays of one lead artist count in full (`SITTING_ARTIST_FULL`), up to the 20th at half, then a quarter; a language counts in full for 12 plays, half to 30, then a quarter; likes, searches, queue/playlist adds, negatives and the song's own affinity always count in full.

**Session intent** (ring of <= 40 events; an action 30 minutes old counts half): per-event pull on the artist (skip -0.40, complete +0.15, like +0.50, unlike -0.30, queue_add +0.35, search_play +0.45; language pull 40% of it, clamped +/-0.6). `deriveIntent` yields skip/completion streaks, artist pull (-1..1), language pull (-0.6..0.6), ids skipped this sitting (hard-filtered until completed or the sitting ends), an energy steer (+/-0.3), and a discovery appetite (-1..1: +0.2 at four straight completions, +0.1 each further up to four; -0.3 at two straight skips, -0.15 each further; -0.1 per search play or hand queue-add down to -0.3). The scorer fades the intent in over its first three events; appetite moves the discovery lean x0.6 and the queue's discovery share x0.15.

### Stages in detail

**Candidate sources:** `related` (catalogue suggestions for the seed (30), recent listens, favourites), `favorite-artist`, `favorite-album`, `album`, `related-artist` (2 artists x 6 songs; cached 30 min), `genre` (skipped while an intent is active), `style`, `proven` (picks finished/liked before), `verified-trend` (<= 6; asked only while the client config says `trendsConnected`, off today, so empty), `trending` (a catalogue search - not evidence, reason says "Popular in the catalogue for your languages"), `intent`, `explore` (Discover mode), `history` (Familiar mode), `rediscovery` (completed > 14 days ago). At most six catalogue requests in flight; identical requests share one fetch; responses reused three minutes (60 kept); after a soft deadline the gather resolves once the pool holds enough and required sources answered; `GATHER_STALL_MS` 900 ms; hard deadline resolves with what settled. Primary source priority (`SOURCE_PRIORITY`): intent, related, favorite-artist, album, favorite-album, related-artist, style, proven, verified-trend, history, rediscovery, genre, explore, trending. Cold start uses pinned languages, then languages of liked songs. Drops blocked, junk, soft-muted, Kid-mode explicit songs before the pool leaves.

**Hard filter order** (`rejectReasonFor`): `invalid`, `junk` (dialogue, background score, jukebox, trailer, promo, ringtone...), `too-short` (known duration < 90 s), then `safetyReasonFor` (`explicit` in Kid mode, `blocked`, `muted-language`, `soft-muted`, `snoozed`), `seed`, `already-queued`, `recently-played`, `heard-before` (Never repeat only), `skipped-this-session`, `off-language`; an `unplayable` song not downloaded is `no-audio`; survivors with the same canonical identity collapse (`duplicate-version`: original over remaster over alternate cut, then more-played). **Canonical identity** (`identityCore.ts`, `songKey`): normalised title + primary artist (NFKC, invisible characters dropped, Latin accents folded, other combining marks kept so Indic vowel signs survive, version decorations and featured credits stripped; `recordingKey` adds the version tag). The Worker uses the same file; both suites run `shared/identity-vectors.json`. **Mix pages:** Your week (`WeeklyMixPage`) and Made for you run their cached songs through `useShelfSafety` when they render, so a hidden song, a Never play artist, a muted language, a soft mute, a snooze or Kid mode applies at once; Play, Shuffle, Save as playlist and the counts use the filtered list, and a mix left with no songs is dropped (`pages/mixSafety.test.tsx`). The Mixes page is gone (it showed the same mixes as Made for you): `/mixes` redirects to `/made-for-you`.

**Scoring weights** (`SCORING_WEIGHTS_VERSION` 1.3.0; features normalised 0-1): mood 0.16, vibe 0.10, language 0.12, dialect 0.08, genre 0.10, energy 0.10, tempo 0.08, artistAffinity 0.14, history 0.10 (subtract), likes 0.10, skips 0.12 (subtract), session 0.12, discovery 0.07, popularity 0.05, freshness 0.04, diversity 0.20, songAffinity 0.12, dayOfWeek 0.04, novelty 0.16 (swing +/-0.08), artistFatigue 0.04, intentArtist 0.18, intentLanguage 0.08, intentEnergy 0.30, intentSkippedSong 0.40 (subtract). Taste-dependent terms are multiplied by `(0.3 + 0.7 x profile confidence) x (0.4 + 0.6 x intensity)`; unknown features score 0; a candidate in a muted language scores -1; scores <= 0 are dropped (`low-score`). Source boosts: intent 0.24, related 0.18, favorite-artist 0.14, favorite-album 0.12, proven 0.12, verified-trend 0.12, rediscovery/history/album/related-artist/style 0.10, explore 0.08, genre 0.07, trending 0.06. `TASTE_WEIGHTS`: `tasteFit` 0.16 (cosine of the 256-slot hashed song vector and the taste vector; recent taste capped at 40% once long-term mass >= 3), `seedRepeat` 0.10, `servedRecently` 0.04 (fallback only). No owner overrides: the `rec-config` rollout, `remoteWeights.ts` / `syncRecConfig` and the Worker's `REC_WEIGHT_TERMS` are gone, so every device runs the default weights. `applyWeightOverrides` / `resetWeightOverrides` in `weights.ts` are a lever for tests only; `activeWeightsVersion()` is still part of every continuation's `alg` stamp.

**Sequencer** (greedy, deterministic): cost = distance from slot target energy x3, mood mismatch x1.2, same lead as previous +4, within last three +1.5, previous lead as featured credit +1, same album +1, energy step beyond 0.35 excess x2.5, decade distance (cap 3) x0.25, pool rank up to +1.0, transition memory -memory x1.5, `lift` shape + "sure" song -1.2, familiar first: sure song -0.9 x (1 - progress), discovery +1.1 x (1 - progress). Arc shapes `steady`, `build`, `wind-down`, `wave`, `lift` (tune's shape, else `lift` after a skip streak >= 2, else a read of the listener: restless -> `lift`, late hours -> `wind-down`, otherwise `steady`). One recording family per stretch. Transition memory (`transitions.ts`, `transitionTracker.ts`): >= 70% heard completed, < 30% skipped.

**Validation** (`validateSequence`): hard filter again, one song per identity; language rule; artist cap ceil(limit/4); no lead artist back to back (counting the seed); discovery allocation floor(share x limit + 0.5); familiar opening (no discovery in slot 1, nor slot 2 with >= 4 songs); style quota; sitting-avoid (artist pull <= -0.6). When nothing else fits, soft rules give way in the order a listener minds least: discovery allocation, familiar opening, artist cap, style quota, sitting-avoid, language-mix rules, and only last the back-to-back rule; each relaxation is reported with its slot. Hard rules never relax. **Plan:** `songs` built inside 8 s (`NEXT_DEADLINE_MS`; 3.5 s when the listener is waiting, `NEXT_URGENT_DEADLINE_MS`), `commit(accepted)` publishes "why this song" lines, the `queued` exposure and seed memory, `topUp(seedNow, n)`, `fallback`, `latencyMs`, `alg` (`PIPELINE_VERSION` 9.1.0 + weights version). **Admission** (`admission.ts`): valid metadata, junk/too-short, explicit in Kid mode, hidden songs/artists, muted languages, soft-muted lead artist, another cut of the playing song, already queued, recent plays, skipped this sitting, heard-before in Never repeat; a Queue Builder plan the listener installs passes only explicit restrictions.

### The exposure ledger (`exposure.ts`)

One memory of what the listener has met, shared by every surface; one row per canonical identity, key `vinax.recs.exposure.v1`, 600 rows (`EXPOSURE_CAP`), 45-day TTL.

| Event | Recorded by | Penalty (freshest) | Decays over | Cools a discovery surface |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `shown` | a Home shelf when it renders | 0.14 | 3 d | 12 h |
| `queued` | the plan's `commit` | 0.24 | 7 d | 2 d |
| `played` | bus `counted` | 0.30 | 10 d | 3 d |
| `completed` | bus `end` at completion ratio | 0.22 | 14 d | 3 d |
| `skipped` | an early manual skip | 0.30 per skip, max 3 | 21 d | 14 d |
| `disliked` | hiding a song | 0.80 | 60 d | 45 d |
| `liked` | favouriting | pays a quarter (`LIKED_FORGIVENESS`); never cooled | - | - |
| `replayed` | explicit "play again" | lifts cooling 7 d | - | - |

The penalty reaches the scorer as `ctx.exposurePenaltyOf` (reason `served`); it never admits a song the hard filter rejects; a plan takes one snapshot. "Clear personalization profile" and "Erase everything" clear it. **Snooze** (`snoozeSong(song, days)`: 7/14/30, default 14) is a hard rule by canonical identity with undo. **Fewer repeats** (`refreshHome({ fewerRepeats: true })`) removes everything with live exposure on discovery surfaces. `recMemory.ts` keeps outcomes (success = liked or natural end completed; miss = manual skip before the play counted or inside the first 30%; forgotten after 60 days; `provenPicks` feed the `proven` source) and seed memory (a continuation's opening songs remembered against the seed identity for 12 hours as `seedRepeatKeys`).

### Never repeat

Settings > Recommendations > **Never repeat** or the Queue page chip (`neverRepeat`, off by default): every song the recommender adds is one the listener has never heard, in any version (remix, remaster, live take count as heard). Hand-queued songs, an installed Queue Builder plan and "Play again" are never held back; turning it on from the chip rebuilds the waiting automatic songs. The **lifetime heard-set** (`heardSet.ts`): canonical keys of every song that produces a `counted` event (whether or not the mode is on), IndexedDB `vinax-heard`, no cap, no expiry; written in one transaction after a 1.5 s pause and when the page hides; looked up through an in-memory Bloom filter (`bloom.ts`, 1% false positives, ~9.6 bits and 7 hashes per song, starts sized for 4,096 songs and doubles); the first load also reads earlier history plays and `play`/`complete` rows once; registered with the admission gate (`registerHeardCheck`); in-memory without IndexedDB; in the backup (category `heard`, <= 200,000 keys; restore only adds) but never the account sync; "Erase everything" deletes it. `heard-before` is a hard rule in `filters.ts`, `admission.ts` and the live planner (`rules.ts`); no relaxation touches it. When fewer than three unheard candidates per slot survive (`NEVER_REPEAT_POOL`) the sources widen (`RecommendationContext.widen`): step 1 more `related-artist` (two pages of each) and three more `genre` pages (`WIDEN_PAGES`), step 2 the `explore` source in the listener's own languages; the live planner uses `planWideLookups` (`nextup/sources.ts`); the trace records `widened-1|2`. Since 1.14.4 the live planner widens this way for every listener, Never repeat or not, whenever a stretch cannot be filled, before it falls back to the liked songs or a shorter cooldown; every other background refill adds the step-1 lookups; liked songs and repeat plays join the pool only as that last fallback, and a liked song's cooldown is 48 hours (was 18). Home's Trending, New releases, Popular and the Daily mix mix the catalogue feed (the same list on every call) with a search page that rotates with the Home generation, in an order shuffled per generation with songs met lately at the back (`features/home/freshRotation.ts`). When nothing is left the plan says `exhausted` and the player shows "You've heard everything we can find here. Try Discover or another language" with a Discover button and stops.

### Settings and switches

**Familiar, Balanced, Discover** (Settings > Recommendations > Discovery, `discoveryMode`; old `exploreMode: true` = Discover):

| | Familiar | Balanced | Discover |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lean | -1 | 0 | +1 |
| Novelty swing on a never-played artist | -0.08 | 0 | +0.08 |
| Share of a stretch open to never-played artists (`DISCOVERY_SHARE`) | 5% | 20% | 45% |
| Extra source | `history` | - | `explore` |

Share clamped 0-50% after appetite. A discovery is a song whose lead artist was never played, or an `explore` candidate; songs fetched for a tune or style are never discoveries.

**Queue languages** (`queueLanguages`: `'mix'` "Your languages" default, or `'one'`): the listener's languages = pinned + profile top three - muted - `unknown`. Mix applies only when the setting is `'mix'` and at least one of them differs from the seed's; otherwise the lock. Mix: hard filter `allowedLanguages` (seed's + listener's; others `off-language`); sequencer `languagePolicy: 'prefer'` (off-target song in one of the listener's languages costs +0.6, or -0.35 after three target-language songs; any other known language +2.5); three hard mix rules - the seed's language fills slots 1 and 2, an off-lead song never follows another, off-lead songs at most floor(limit/2) - relaxed only if no candidate satisfies them (`language-mix`). Lock: `'lock'` on the seed's language; validation relaxes only if fewer than three songs remain (`language-lock`). Songs with no/`unknown` language pass under both. "Switch language" moves a lock for that stretch.

**Tunes, styles** (`tune.ts`): energetic, chill, romantic, melody, beats (`mass`), devotional, heartbreak, classics, new (`fresh`), same language, switch language, surprise, styles `dj` and `folk`. Pin a mood is gone (1.16): its chips became the Tune this queue row in Now Playing's Up next tab, and the session vector always comes from the rolling window. `tuneQueue(intent)` keeps played, current and hand-queued songs, replaces the rest; an active intent adds the `intent` source (`tuneSearchQuery`, a two-word phrase prefixed with the queue's language), adjusts scores (`tuneScoreAdjust` +0.35 for the asked-for mood + nudges) and sets the arc (energetic/beats -> `build`; chill, melody, romantic, heartbreak, devotional -> `wind-down`; surprise -> `wave`). `style.ts` reads `dj`, `folk`, `devotional` from words in title/album/subtitle/artists (strong) and genre/mood (weak); `sessionStyle` decides; active style: required `style` source, `STYLE_WEIGHTS` (+0.4 word match, +0.25 metadata match, -0.3 off-style, 0.1 source boost), validation keeps >= ceil(0.8 x limit) in style (`STYLE_MIN_SHARE`), one remix per song per sitting.

### Queue rules in the player (`store/playerStore.ts`)

One continuation adds five songs (`NEXT_BATCH`); requested when a song starts with <= 2 remaining, provided autoplay (or radio) is on, follow mode off and repeat off; one request per queue version (a queue change cancels; late results discarded); additions pass admission and are marked automatic. **Build every queue** (`djTakeover`, default; with Autoplay on): tapping a song makes it the seed. Smart Queue (`features/queue/SmartQueue.tsx`) is one switch over `autoplay && djTakeover`. The player tracks recommender-appended vs listener-queued ids: "Add to queue" inserts right after the current song and the songs already added by hand (1.15, as Spotify does), so it plays ahead of the rest of the album or playlist and of the automatic songs; "Play next" directly after the current. **Shuffle (1.15)** reorders the queue itself, so the queue shows the real play order and Back returns to the song before: turning it on shuffles the list songs still to come (added songs stay first, automatic songs last); turning it off puts them back in the list's original order (`listOrder`, kept with the ownership record), carrying on from the last list song heard (`store/queueOrder.ts`). A list started with shuffle on plays the tapped song first and the rest shuffled; the Shuffle play buttons call `playQueue(songs, 0, { shuffle: true })` (shuffle on, random first song); repeat-all reshuffles each time round. **Playing from (1.15):** `context` (persisted) is what the listener pressed play on: `playQueue`'s `opts.from`, else `inferContext` from the route and the page title ("<name> · VinaX"); radio is "<song> radio". Now Playing's "Playing from" and the Queue page's "Next from:" read it; re-plan/tune replace only the automatic tail; "Keep this song" turns an automatic entry into the listener's; `regenerateAutoTail`. Adaptive re-plan (`adaptive.ts`): two consecutive automatic skips re-sequence the remaining tail (>= 3 songs) with `lift` and a few favourites in the playing song's language under a language lock (90 s cooldown `REPLAN_COOLDOWN_MS`). Radio (`features/radio/radioSeeds.ts`, `pages/RadioPage.tsx`): `startRadio` puts <= `RADIO_SEED_MAX` (5) seeds in the queue as the listener's list, sets radio mode (continuations run even with Autoplay off) and an optional tune.

**Podcast episodes** (`isEpisode`, `features/podcasts/episode.ts`) teach the taste profile nothing, since the host is not an artist: `learnsNothing` in `personalization/updater.ts` skips artist and language bumps, likes, the session window and session intent (an episode left early sends no session skip either), while its play, complete and skip events still go to the listen-event log, so Your VinaX counts them. An episode is never an Up Next seed (`startAnchor`, `addAnchorSeed`; `readAnchor` started from one has no seeds): when the queue held only episodes, Up Next adds no music and says "That was the last episode in your queue" (`EPISODES_END` in `services/nextup/engine.ts`; `warmNextUp` returns early too), and a music session with an episode queued carries on from the music.

### Home shelves

`buildRecommendations(ctx)` gathers, filters, ranks without a seed, moves cooling identities behind, assembles shelves in `mixes.ts`; memoised ten minutes. Home generation (`features/home/homeRefresh.ts`): queries carry the generation in the key (30-minute TTL `HOME_TTL_MS`); it moves only on explicit refresh or opening Home > 30 minutes after it started; safety changes rebuild nothing (lists filter at render, `features/home/blocks/shared.tsx`). **Repetition rule** (`surfacePolicy.ts`): `resume` surfaces keep heard songs; `personal` (Made For You, Daily mix, Because you liked) move heard/shown to the back; `discovery` (trending, new releases, popular picks, mood shelves, feed) remove heard and move shown to the back (removed under "fewer repeats"); the automatic queue uses hard rules and the exposure penalty. Popular picks (`trendingForYou.ts`) come from the catalogue's trending results only. Home's own order (`homeOrder.ts`): time of day, taps (`homeTaps.ts`, 14-day half-life), outcomes (`homeSignals.ts`), genre affinity; each block moves at most three places; `sessionHomeOrder` fixes the order per session. De-duplication (`shelfLedger.ts`): a shelf drops songs already claimed earlier (by id and canonical identity) and keeps at least six if it had six (`SHELF_FLOOR`). Taste profile page (`features/taste-profile/useTasteInsights.ts`: skip rate, top moods, new-to-you share; the last-7-days trend is gone, and `mostReplayed` stays only for the hero's cover collage) and Similar artists (`useSimilarArtists.ts`: 3 most-played artists). The Taste profile page shows the weights under one header, **What shapes your mixes** (Languages in your mixes, Artists in your mixes, When you listen); its Most replayed and Last 7 days sections were dropped because Your VinaX has Most played and the weekly report, and the two pages link to each other (header pills).

**Your Week** (`/weekly`, `features/weekly/useWeeklyMix.ts`) is the weekly discovery mix, the `discover-weekly` mix "New to you this week" with 30 songs. It loads the lifetime heard-set first (dynamic import), then calls `buildRecommendations` with the Monday ISO-week salt and `widen: 2`, which adds the `explore` source in the listener's own languages; `ctx.widen` is part of the engine's ten-minute cache key, so this wider build never reaches Home. `weekDiscoveries()` (`mixes.ts`) leaves out songs in the history, the taste profile or the heard-set; a first pass takes only songs whose lead artist the listener does not know (`listenerFamiliarity()` in `scoring.ts`, which `buildScoringFrame` uses too), a second pass tops up from `explore` picks by any artist, at most three songs per lead artist. The week is cached in `vinax.weekly.v1` as `{ v: 2, week, langs, songs }`, so an older cache (the first 30 songs of every mix) is rebuilt. `withYourWeek()` puts the built week into Made for you's "New to you this week" (in `useRecommendations` and in the cached `['mixes']` queries), so both show the same list. The Charts sidebar tile reads "Your Week <n>" and "Songs new to you · refreshes every Monday".

### Catalogue recommendation routes

`backend/worker/functions/_lib/recs.ts`: anonymous, store nothing, separate from the engine; `services/recs/similar.ts` calls them for "Songs like this", Discover 5 and a playlist's Recommended songs (`features/library/playlistRecs.ts` -> `recommendFromSeeds`: up to 5 of the playlist's songs as seeds, one per artist first and a different set on each Refresh, 20 asked for with the shown songs then the playlist as `exclude`, the first 10 still safe shown; `RecommendedSongs.tsx` fetches only within 300px of the screen and filters through `useShelfSafety`), resolving each track through `getSong` and falling back to the catalogue's own suggestions. Candidates: the catalogue's similar-songs list (`reason: "similar"`), then songs the catalogue **credits** to the seed's lead artist (`same_artist`); at most `max(2, ceil(limit/5))` per artist.

| Route | Query | Rate limit | 200 answer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/recommendations/similar/:songId` | `songId` matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{2,40}$`; `limit` 1-30 (20); `languages` <= 6 | 30 burst, 30/min | `{ seed, tracks, source: "catalogue" }`, `public, max-age=600, s-maxage=3600` |
| `GET /api/recommendations?seeds=...` | 1-5 song ids; `limit`, `languages`, `exclude` <= 100 ids | 10 burst, 10/min | `{ seeds, tracks, source: "catalogue" }`, `private, max-age=600`; seeds take turns |

Each track: `id, title, artist, artists (<= 4), album, language, year, durationSec, reason, reasonText`. Errors: `400 invalid_song_id | bad_request`, `404 song_not_found`, `429 rate_limited` (+ `retry-after`), `502 catalogue_unavailable`, `405` for POST.

### Debugging and changing safely

Developer breakdown on with `?debug=recs`, `localStorage` `vinax.debug.recs` = `1`, or a dev build (`store/recsDebugStore.ts`; panel `features/recommendation/RecsDebugPanel.tsx`, lazy): trace, selected songs with every component, passed over, rejected (<= 80 with rule and stage). Listener-facing: "Why this song?" (`explanations.ts`; a `served` reason means the ledger or seed memory held the song back; "AI pick - <reason>").

| If you change | This must pass |
| --- | --- |
| Any weight, source, filter, sequencer or validation rule | Unit tests beside the modules (`services/recommendation/*.test.ts`) and `node scripts/eval-recs.mjs --no-baseline` |
| A key in the weight table | `weightEffects.test.ts`, `weightOverrides.test.ts` |
| Repetition (ledger, seed memory, surface policy) | `repetition.accept.test.ts`, `exposure.test.ts` |
| Never repeat | `heardSet.test.ts`, `bloom.test.ts`, `admission.test.ts`, `filters.test.ts`, `nextup/rules.test.ts`, `engine.test.ts`, and the 500-song sitting in `repetition.accept.test.ts` (zero repeats by canonical identity) |
| Canonical identity | `identityContract.test.ts` + the Worker's suite (shared vectors) |
| Queue languages / next five | `nextFive.test.ts`, `engine.test.ts`, `validation.test.ts`, `sequencerPolicy.test.ts`; the `mixed-queue` fixture |
| Styles | `style.test.ts`, `engine.style.test.ts` |

Lessons kept: `servedRecently` (0.04) is a leftover fallback - the ledger's penalties (0.14-0.80) act; adjacency relaxes last (the earlier order shipped runs of one artist in Familiar mode; do not reorder the tiers without the evaluation); `trending` is a catalogue search, not a chart; Home de-duplicates through `shelfLedger.ts`; `backend/worker/functions/api/recommendations.ts` ("songs like these") is not part of this engine.

## Offline evaluation of next-song selection

A harness in `frontend/eval/` runs the real recommendation engine against synthetic listeners with the network replaced, reporting rule compliance, diversity mechanics, fallback behaviour and latency, so a change can be compared with the engine before it in a few minutes. It does not measure enjoyment. No npm script and no workflow; on demand from `frontend/`:

```sh
node scripts/eval-recs.mjs --no-baseline            # the working tree only (~3 minutes)
node scripts/eval-recs.mjs --baseline <commit>      # working tree, then that commit, side by side
npx vitest run --config eval/vitest.config.ts       # the harness alone
npx tsc --noEmit -p eval/tsconfig.json              # type-check the harness
```

Flags: `--no-baseline`, `--baseline <commit>` (default `7c4e2f5` can no longer load - the baseline must contain `frontend/src/services/recommendation/recMemory.ts`; check with `git cat-file -e <commit>:frontend/src/services/recommendation/recMemory.ts`), `--quick` (3 salts, 3 latency runs), `--salts <n>` (12), `--latency-runs <n>` (8), `--cap-ms <n>` (12000), `--out <dir>` (`frontend/eval/reports`), `--report-only`. Unknown flag exits 2. Passed on as `EVAL_PIPELINE`, `EVAL_REF`, `EVAL_OUT`, `EVAL_SALTS`, `EVAL_LATENCY_RUNS`, `EVAL_LATENCY_CAP_MS`, `EVAL_ASSERT`, `EVAL_SRC`. Writes `recs-eval-current.json`, `recs-eval-baseline.json`, `recs-eval.json`, `recs-eval.md` (git-ignored, with `eval/.cache/baseline-<commit>/`).

**How it works:** fixtures (`buildFixtures(now)`, fictional songs in Telugu, Hindi, Tamil, Punjabi, Malayalam and Latin scripts; `EVAL_FIXTURES_VERSION` 1.4.0 - bump when a fixture changes, never compare across versions) include `cold`, `warm`, `familiar`, `discover`, per-language fixtures, `mixed`, `mixed-queue`, `prefs`, `skips` (streak of three), `partial-outage`, `offline`, `sparse`, `versions`, `small-catalogue`, `kid-mode`, `muted-languages`, `hidden-artists`, `soft-muted`, `dj-session`, `folk-session`, `deep-sources`, `served`, `memory`, `unplayable`, `sparse-history`; two more serve latency only. Only `@/services/api` (catalogue client) and `@/services/queryClient` are mocked, one level below `candidates.ts` so candidate-stage rules stay measured. A sitting is four continuations of five songs (seed of each later one is `queue[length - 2]`), one run per salt; rules are re-written in `eval/lib/rules.ts` (only song identity is shared, pinned by `shared/identity-vectors.json`).

**Metrics:** hard-rule violations (Kid mode explicit, muted language, hidden artist/song, soft-muted lead, same id/work as last 20 plays or already queued, skipped this sitting, unplayable), off-language under the lock (excused when < 3 eligible in-language candidates remained), mix rules (`off-language`, `language-opening`, `language-run`), same lead back to back / within three, identity repeats, artist coverage, discovery share vs allocation (familiar 0.05, balanced 0.2, discover 0.45), familiar-first compliance, pickers/fallbacks/relaxations, style continuity, retrieval depth, declared-taste agreement, pushed-away artists, served songs queued, seed-memory replay, latency p50/p95 (queue-ready and final) across `instant-sources`, `slow-sources` (140-900 ms), `source-throws`, `source-never-settles` (+ `-large-pool`, `-urgent` at `deadlineMs: 3500`).

**The gate** (`recs.eval.ts`, three assertions): `quality.overall.batches > 0`; `deterministic === true` (the `warm` fixture with salt 1 queues the same ids twice); `hardViolations === 0` (skipped when `EVAL_ASSERT=0`, which the runner sets for a baseline). The runner exits 0 when the current pipeline's run exits 0, 1 otherwise (or when no report was written). Everything else is a measurement to read. Gate failures: `hard-rule violations` > 0 -> `quality.overall.violations`/`perFixture` in the JSON; `reproducible NO` -> `determinismDiff` (module-level memory surviving `localStorage.clear()`, unfrozen clock, randomness not derived from the salt); `No report written` -> unresolved import in the tree under test.

**Changing the engine safely:** run the unit tests (`npx vitest run src/services/recommendation`), run the evaluation before and after and read every summary line for unintended movement, run `repetition.accept.test.ts` for repetition changes, bump the fixture/harness version if a fixture or metric changes. **The repetition acceptance test** (`services/recommendation/repetition.accept.test.ts`, part of the normal unit run) uses 120 playable songs by 24 artists and `planNextSongs` for 20 at a time, committing as the player does: second continuation from the same seed introduces >= 12 of 20 new songs (also after a reload with the ledger kept); no identity repeats in a continuation over three salts; no lead back to back, no artist above ceil(n/3), >= 6 artists; muted language/hidden song/hidden artist never ship; an exhausted pool returns fewer songs; queue-surfaced songs are in the ledger and Home-shown songs cost the queue (< 10 return); a liked song may come back; Familiar's discovery share < 0.1; a single-artist request is respected. The evaluation cannot see repetition across sittings or surfaces (every continuation is called with the whole queue excluded, `applyFixture` clears `localStorage` before each continuation except `memory`, no metric compares sittings), enjoyment, a real catalogue (pools of 6-104 songs), audio features, the profile updater and admission gate, or field latency. Known figures (fixtures 1.3.0/1.4.0 era, not gated): discovery share runs about twice the allocation (35.8% vs 17.9%) because the budget holds only while familiar candidates remain; same-lead-back-to-back 36 (24 avoidable); the seed-memory replay reads "3 of 3 without memory" (expected: nothing to remember without a commit); `served songs queued` reads 27.5% (it read 37.5% until fixtures 1.4.0 was deepened).

**Real outcomes** come only from two consent-gated events (`services/analytics/recTelemetry.ts`): `rec_served` (once per automatic continuation: `alg`, `picker`, `fallback`, `latencyMs`, `n`, `discovery`, `languageViolations`, `distinctArtists`, `relaxed`, `exp`) and `rec_outcome` (when an automatic entry's playback ends: `alg`, `picker`, `pos`, `heardSec`, `durationSec`, `outcome` complete|skip|early_skip|partial, `liked`, `exp`). Neither carries a song or song id. Client rate limit: bucket `REC_BUCKET` 12, one more every `REC_REFILL_MS` 5 s, outbox `REC_OUTBOX_CAP` 40, `REC_SESSION_CAP` 400 per session. `planNextSongs` calls `decideRecVariant('rec-next-songs')` on every plan; arms set the taste-fit weight (`REC_TASTE_ARMS` in `engine.ts`: `taste-0.15`, `taste-0.18`; `control` and unknown names keep the shipped weight); create the experiment in the console. **Reading an A/B result:** pick one primary metric first (completion rate = complete / (complete + skip + early_skip + partial)); aggregate per device then compare device-rate distributions (bootstrap over devices); report an interval (Wilson); ~1,500 outcomes (~100 listeners) per arm detects 5 points, ~4,200 (~300) 3 points, ~37,000 (~2,500) 1 point at 40% completion, 95% confidence, 80% power; fix the reading date in advance; only listeners who turned telemetry on are in the data; watch `rec_served` guardrails; rate limiting loses the most restless sittings first, biasing skip rates down.

## Design system

### Design system v2

The 2026-11 UI rebuild put every screen on one system: semantic tokens, a small set of primitives and one motion language. The older names further down (`--vx-*`, `--ink-*`, `.btn-*`) still work, and they read the same tokens.

**Tokens** (`frontend/src/styles/tokens.css`, loaded right after `index.css`)
- Each token says what a value is *for*. Each is built on the palette channels (`--ink-*`, `--ember-*`, `--tide-*`), which the app style sets. So every component follows the look without an override.
  - Colour: `--surface-0..4`, `--text-1..4`, `--accent`, `--accent-text`, `--accent-soft`, `--accent-2`, `--line-1..3`, and `--positive`, `--warning`, `--danger`, `--info`.
  - Type: `--type-hero` … `--type-overline`. Space: `--space-*`.
  - Shape: `--radius-xs` … `--radius-2xl` and `--radius-pill`, plus `--radius-control`, `--radius-tile`, `--radius-input` and `--radius-art`, which each app style reshapes.
  - Depth: `--elev-1..4`. Layers: `--z-*`.
  - Motion: `--dur-instant|quick|base|slow|page`, `--ease-standard|enter|exit|spring|bounce` (the springs are `linear()` curves, with a cubic fallback), `--press-scale` and `--delay-hint`. Under either reduced-motion switch, every `--dur-*` is 0 and `--press-scale` is 1.
- **The style guard** (`src/__tests__/styleGuard.test.ts`) keeps raw values from creeping back:
  - no raw `999px` or `9999px` pill anywhere;
  - `ui.css` stays token-only;
  - every other stylesheet may only *lose* raw hex colours and raw `ms` durations against a per-file baseline. Lower a baseline when you remove raw values, never raise it; add a token instead.

**Primitives and helpers**
- `src/components/ui/`: `Button` (primary, secondary, tonal, ghost and a tinted danger), `IconButton`, `Switch`, `Slider`, `Tabs` (segmented or underline, with a sliding indicator and arrow keys), `Card`/`cardClass`, `ListRow`, `Badge`, `Tooltip`, `Dialog` (built on `Sheet`) and `Spinner`. Their styles are in `styles/ui.css`. The legacy `.btn-*` classes share their timing, radius and spring press.
- `src/utils/motion.ts`:
  - `animate(el, keyframes, { duration, easing })` is a WAAPI call driven by the tokens; it is `null` under reduced motion.
  - `withViewTransition(update)` takes an update; put `flushSync` inside it.
  - `stagger(i)` gives the delay for item `i` of a reveal.
- `/design` (`pages/DesignPage.tsx`, `features/design/`) is the living style guide.
  - It shows every token with measured contrast, every primitive, the motion curves and the player's shared-element move.
  - Its preview bar (reduced motion) changes only `<html>` while the page is open.
  - The page is unlinked, `noindex` and on demand. `e2e/design-system.spec.ts` gates it.

**Motion**
- **Page changes are View Transitions.** `utils/pageTransitions.ts` wraps `router.navigate`; Back replays the transition.
  - Only the page area (`vx-page`) moves. It arrives with the app style's own entrance (`--tpl-enter`). The player artwork (`vx-np-art`) grows into Now Playing's.
  - There is no transition for replace navigations, moves inside one screen (`/search*`, `/vinaxai*`), reduced motion, or browsers without the API.
  - Names are set only while a transition runs (`.vx-vt-on`). A named element is a backdrop root, so a permanent name would stop the glass chrome blurring.
  - Gated by `e2e/page-transitions.spec.ts`.
- **The chrome.** Buttons and icons grow a touch (1.04) on hover; a new song slides into the player bar; play and pause glyphs spring in when they swap.
- **The player.** A new cover eases in (but not on opening Now Playing), its tab panels cross-fade, Up next and Queue rows arrive in a stagger (`--i`, capped at 8), and the seek thumb grows while held (`e2e/player-motion.spec.ts`).
- **The right-click menu** loads its panel (`ContextMenuPanel.tsx`) on first use; only the listener ships in the first load. A right-click on selected text, in a field or with Shift gets the browser's menu; on touch the browser's menu is held back only on tap-and-hold surfaces (`[data-deter-context]`, `.vx-bottom-chrome`, `.vx-sidebar`), which alone are not selectable, so reading pages can be copied (`ContextMenu.test.tsx`).

**Content rules for lists** (the catalogue repeats itself)
- `utils/songLine.ts` gives a song its title, film or album, and artist, each said once:
  - a "(From …)" tag moves from the title to the album;
  - a tagged album name gives up its film;
  - "Artists - Album" subtitles are split;
  - an "artist" that only repeats the album is dropped.
- `SongRow`, the chart rows, the queue and Up next all read `songLine`. Where a list has no album column, the album follows the artists after a "·".
- `utils/artistTagline.ts` reduces "Artist • 9487361 Listeners" to a real tagline or nothing.
- Artists without a photo get a monogram (initials on a hue from their name) in `MediaCard`.

**App styles, VinaX AI, the console**
- **App styles.** `templates/base.css` feeds each style's dials into the tokens: `--tpl-r-*` into `--radius-*`, and `--tpl-press` into `--press-scale` where motion is allowed. The look restates the app's radius scale (`--radius-xs..2xl`) in `templates/vinax.css`, not in `index.css`, because the console copies `index.css`'s scale.
- **VinaX AI.** `vinaxai.css` reads the same tokens; `--vx-ai-*` are aliases; the last section of `vinaxai.css` gives it its own warm look (the Claude look; dark only since 1.15). Live voice's controls scale down to 44px so all six fit a phone.
- **The owner console** shares the colour-free tokens through the generated `public/admin/css/tokens.css` (`npm run gen:admin-tokens`, checked by `adminTokens.test.ts`) and keeps its own colours.

**Left as they are, on purpose**
- **Old names stay.** The `--vx-*` names and the palette channels stay because hundreds of rules read them and they resolve to the same values.
- **Dialogs stay.** The roughly 20 hand-built dialogs (VinaX AI's among them) already trap focus and close on Escape and Back; they move to `Dialog` when their screens are next reworked.
- **Google's button keeps Google's colours.** In `signin.css` it is Google's branding, the only raw colours left on the sign-in screens.

**Sign-in and public pages**
- `auth.css` (`/login`, `/signup`, `/forgot-password`), `signin.css` (the email forms, the account row, the sign-in sheet) and `public.css` (the pages signed-out visitors land on) read the tokens. 1.15: the sign-in pages are a #121212 card on a grey-to-black page (no card on a phone), outlined fields that turn white on focus.
- The main action ("Sign in", "Send code", "Set password") is the green pill, like `<Button>`. While the form is empty it is the same green, quieter.

### How the layers stack

| Layer | Switch on `<html>` | Lives in | May change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Base tokens and frame | - | `frontend/src/styles/index.css`, `shell.css`, `features.css`, `pages/*.css` | Everything, as defaults |
| App style | `data-template='<id>'` | `styles/templates/` | Token values, `--tpl-*` dials, its own shell/card layout rules |
| Accessibility | `.hc`, `.reduce-motion` (and `.dark`, always on) | `index.css`, `templates/base.css`, `tokens.css` | High contrast; durations to 0 |

`main.tsx` imports `index.css`, `shell.css`, `features.css`, `templates/index.css`; page stylesheets load later with their lazy page.

### Token contract

Components read tokens; colour tokens hold space-separated RGB channels (`rgb(var(--ink-900))`). `--ink-950/900/850/800/700` (surfaces, darkest to raised; `--ink-900` is the page canvas), `--ink-100`..`--ink-500` (text, strongest to faintest), `--ember-300`..`--ember-600` (accent ramp), `--tide-400/500` (secondary accent), `--vx-on-accent`, `--art` (playing artwork colour), `--vx-radius-control` 10px / `--vx-radius-card` 14px / `--vx-radius-panel` 20px, `--radius-sm`..`--radius-pill`, `--vx-control-h` 40px, `--vx-control-h-sm` 36px, `--vx-tile-h` 48px, `--vx-topbar-h` 64px (phones `calc(max(8px, env(safe-area-inset-top)) + 48px)`), `--ease-calm` (`cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)`), `--vx-ease-out`. Tailwind colour names map to the same variables (`frontend/tailwind.config.ts`).

### Themes and glass (1.15.1)

**Theme:** Settings > Appearance > Theme and VinaX AI's settings > Appearance offer **System** (the default; follows the device, live), **Light** and **Dark**. The ramps for both live in `templates/vinax.css` (`html[data-template='vinax']:root` dark, `html.light[data-template='vinax']:root` light: white window, #f8f8f8 panels, #121212 type, a deeper green `20 138 60` that clears 3:1 as a ring and 4.5:1 as text on white, black labels on it). `index.css`'s `html.light` block re-pitches only the tokens not built on the ramps (danger, hairlines, lyric tokens, solid surfaces). There is no Black (AMOLED) theme and no accent picker. VinaX AI's Claude look has its light palette again (the last section of `vinaxai.css`).

**Glass:** the window (`.vx-shell`) is a mesh of the playing artwork's colour (`--art`) and a little green, with a slow 48 s drift on `.vx-shell::before` (off under either reduced-motion switch).
